### T46. Simulator sharing for the SwiftUI screenshot suite

The SwiftUI screenshot suite (`packages/visual/test/swiftui.test.ts`, T36) installs one app id on one shared iOS Simulator, so two worktrees running it at once overwrite each other's screenshots and fail with false pixel diffs. The creator approved both remedies, chosen by a setting:

- **shared** (default): one simulator for everyone, with a machine-wide lock so runs from different worktrees take turns; a run waits for the lock with a bounded timeout and a clear message, and a stale lock left by a dead process is taken over.
- **own:** each worktree gets its own simulator device, created on first use from the same device type and runtime and named after the worktree, so runs never share state; the suite says how to delete these devices.

The setting is an environment variable read by the suite (a test-harness choice, not a Weft tool option, so no `weft.json` key), documented in the suite's README and `AGENTS.md`. Done when two runs started at once in two worktrees pass in both modes, the lock and the device naming have tests, and the default run on one worktree behaves as before.

Result: `WEFT_SIMULATOR` (`shared` by default, `own`; anything else is an error) is read by the suite. `packages/visual/src/simulator.ts` holds the mode parsing, the own-mode device name `weft-visual-<worktree folder>` (the prefix keeps shared mode off these devices and lets the README list them), the device selection and the lock. The lock is Node's fs only, no dependency: the holder's pid goes into a temp file that is hard-linked to `~/Library/Caches/weft-visual/simulator.lock` (atomic, never half-written, fails while held); a waiter warns once with the holder's pid and the lock path, waits up to 30 minutes (a loaded run takes over twenty) and then fails naming the file; a lock whose pid is gone or unreadable is taken over by renaming it away and checking what was renamed. The cache folder, not `os.tmpdir()`, because simulators belong to the user while `TMPDIR` differs between sessions and sandboxes. The suite builds the app first, then takes the lock for the whole suite (shared: one lock; own: one lock per device, so two runs of one worktree also take turns), creates the own device with `xcrun simctl create` on first use and reuses it, and shuts down what it booted and releases the lock in `afterAll`. `test/simulator.test.ts` (16 tests, in the Node project of `vitest.config.ts`) covers mode parsing, naming, device selection and the lock: release, wait, timeout message, stale and unreadable takeover, no takeover of a live holder, release leaving another holder's lock alone, six contenders on one stale lock. Proof of the parallel runs, each pair started at the same moment from two worktrees of this branch: own mode, both 22 of 22 passing with overlapping runs (no waiting); shared mode, both 22 of 22 passing, one after the other (lock held by one pid until it released, then by the other's) on a stand-in shared device. The real shared device ("iPhone 17") was also used by other agents' unlocked runs while this was tested, which gave false diffs in every attempt that overlapped one; a solo default run and the full check on it pass. `packages/visual/README.md` and `AGENTS.md` document the setting, the lock and how to list (`xcrun simctl list devices | grep weft-visual-`) and delete (`xcrun simctl delete <name>`) the devices. The full check exits 0. Open questions for the creator: runs on branches without this change still use the shared device unlocked and can disturb a locked run; the suite never deletes an own device, so one stays behind for each removed worktree until someone deletes it.

### T47. Static page parity with the generated components

`weft html` pages and the generated React and SolidJS components show the same data (T43), but `packages/visual` listed every screen in `STATIC_PAGE_DIFFERS` and most in `STATIC_TREE_DIFFERS`. Done when every entry of `STATIC_TREE_DIFFERS` is gone, `STATIC_PAGE_DIFFERS` keeps only screens with a stated, unavoidable reason, changed baselines and snapshots are reviewed, and the full check exits 0. No pixel tolerance may be added.

Result: the static page now matches the components, not a shared stylesheet, because the components, the reference renderer and the Figma and Penpot round trips are already compared with each other and only the page was the outlier. In `crates/weft-web/src/html.rs` a `text` is a block `div` (`data-weft-kind="text"`), a control's caption is an `aria-hidden` span behind the control's `aria-label`, a link with no usable `href` is `role="link" tabindex="0"`, a radio group is a `div` instead of a `fieldset`, a field error is a `div`, the field and toggle wrappers lost their classes, and inline pieces join without whitespace, as JSX writes them. The page stylesheet keeps only layout the components carry inline, plus `a { display: inline-block }`. The visual suite also gives the component and reference frames the `weft css-tokens` stylesheet, as a host does; without it the page spaced stacks by gap tokens where React did not. Both `STATIC_*` lists are gone: all 16 screens match React pixel for pixel (same comparator, no tolerance) and in the accessibility tree. HTML snapshots (62) and the web and static page baselines of the changed screens were retaken and reviewed.

### T53. Shared base stylesheet for web targets

T47 made the static page match the generated React and SolidJS components by dropping the page's own styling. Both now render unstyled: a caption sits flush against its control, fields have no spacing, and a link without `href` does not look like a link. The creator chose one Weft base stylesheet for every web target, so all of them look good and stay identical.

- **The stylesheet.** One minimal, token-driven base stylesheet:
  - captions above fields, with spacing between fields;
  - checkbox, switch and radio rows;
  - link appearance, including `role="link"`;
  - buttons, stacks and the other kinds that need it.
  - Values come from the project's tokens (spacing, colours, typography), with fallbacks when a token is absent, and follow light and dark modes (T45).
- **Where it ships.** Written by `weft css-tokens` (or a sibling command, decided in the plan) for React and SolidJS hosts, and inlined by `weft html`.
- **Where it applies.** The visual harness gives it to every web frame, as it now does with `weft-tokens.css`. The reference renderer and the Figma/Penpot round trips are adjusted wherever they are compared with the components.
- **Accessibility.** The tree stays as T47 left it.

Done when:
- the static page and the components still match pixel for pixel and in the accessibility tree;
- the reviewed baselines show captions above fields, spacing and visible links;
- SPEC and docs describe the stylesheet;
- the full check exits 0.

Result: one base stylesheet, `crates/weft-web/src/base.css`, shared by every web target. `weft html` inlines it in the page's `<style>` between the tokens and the layout, and `weft css-base` (a sibling of `weft css-tokens`, same `--out-dir` and `export.css.outDir`) writes it as `weft-base.css` for React and SolidJS hosts to link after `weft-tokens.css`. It does not depend on the project: every value is a `var(--weft-…, fallback)` with the default token's value as the fallback (a test checks that), and borders and muted text mix `currentColor`, so light and dark follow the page; it sets no page colour or font. It covers screen, form, section and dialog (a column with a gap), field captions above their controls with the error underneath, checkbox, switch (a track and thumb) and radio rows, radio groups, links including `role="link"`, buttons (`primary` and `danger` filled, disabled dimmed), tabs, alerts and tones, the footer and actions slots, tables, lists and menus. It selects only elements and attributes that the page, the components and the reference renderer all write, so nothing in the generated markup changed and the accessibility tree is as T47 left it. The visual suite gives every web frame `weft css-tokens` plus `weft css-base`; the reference renderer's whole page (`renderPage`) takes the sheet through a new `styles` option; the Figma and Penpot round trips and SwiftUI needed no change. The sheet showed one importer gap that unstyled pages hid: the React and SolidJS importers read a field's conditional error message as separate text outside the field, and now read it back as the field's bound `error`. Every corpus screen still matches React pixel for pixel (no tolerance) and in the accessibility tree; the web and static page baselines of all 19 screens (the corpus and the light and dark example) and the 64 changed HTML snapshots (each gains only the stylesheet) were retaken and reviewed.

### T54. Run only the checks a change affects

The full check took 5-7 minutes and agents ran it many times while they iterated; the creator asked for nextest and for runs of only what the changed files affect. Required: Rust runs the changed crates and their dependents (nextest `rdeps()`); TypeScript runs the affected projects and, inside one, the tests Vitest can relate to the change; one command (documented in `AGENTS.md` and the README as the iteration loop); the full check stays the merge gate; a change set that cannot be computed, or a changed lockfile, toolchain or workspace configuration, falls back to the full run and says why. Done when the command runs the expected subset for a leaf-crate change, a `weft-core` change, a TypeScript-only change and a docs-only change, the selection logic has tests, and the full check exits 0.

Result: `moon run root:changed` (`tooling/changed.ts`, `-- --dry-run` prints the plan) diffs the working tree against the merge base with `main`, then runs the Rust track (`root:rust-test -- -E 'rdeps(=crate) | ...'`, clippy `-p` the same closure, `cargo fmt --check`) and the TypeScript track (moon targets, split into those run with `--changed <merge base>` and those run whole) in parallel. Moon's own `--affected` could not do it: the inherited `test` task declares `/packages/**/*`, so one changed line in one package selects every test, and the crate globs make every task depend on one crate edit. `tooling/select.ts` is therefore a pure function over moon's project and task data (`moon query`), `cargo metadata --no-deps` and the changed files: a task is affected by a changed file that matches its declared inputs, except the two coarse globs, which the package graph (package.json dependencies plus paths that sources name, one step) and the crate closure replace; tasks that depend on an affected task (the generated module, the CLI, the Xcode bundle) are affected too. Vitest `--changed` narrows a project only when every cause is an import of a TypeScript module; a data file, fixture or shared input runs it whole. The full run is the fallback for a lockfile, `mise.toml`, `.moon/`, `moon.yml`, the workspace files, root `Cargo.toml`, `tsconfig.json`, `vitest.config.ts`, `clippy.toml`, a missing merge base or a file in `crates/` that belongs to no crate. `tooling/test/select.test.ts` (39 tests over a fixture workspace) runs in the new `tooling` project. No new dependency; the sources and measurements are in `research.md` section 14. The example change sets, each run on a machine at load average 100-360 from other agents, so the times are upper bounds: docs-only runs nothing (13 s, all of it selection); a TypeScript-only change in `render-react` ran 12 projects with `--changed` plus lint and typecheck (1 m 23 s); a change in `weft-swiftui` ran 74 nextest tests (`weft-swiftui`, `weft-cli`, `weft-snapshots`), clippy on those three crates, `visual:test` and `xcode-plugin:test` (19 m, of which 13 m is the Swift type-check tests of that crate); a change in `weft-core` reached every crate and every TypeScript check (all 614 Rust tests, 20 test projects, runtimes, typecheck), no saving, as the card says; its one failure was a 120 s property-test timeout under the load, and the same suite passed in the full check. The full check exits 0 (8 m 29 s under the same load).

### T57. Cache the mcp test task

The `mcp` package's `test` task was never served from moon's cache. Vitest writes `node_modules/.vite/.../results.json`, which falls inside the task's `**/*` input, so the hash changed after every run and `moon ci` always re-ran the tests. Required: find the cause in the inherited `test` task or the package's own config and keep Vitest's cache output out of the inputs (or move it with `cacheDir`); check every other package for the same problem. Done when a second `moon run :test` with no changes is fully cached, a source change still re-runs the affected tests, and the full check exits 0.

Result: the cause was wider than the card said: the inherited `test` task's `/packages/**/*` hashes every package's `node_modules`, so any package's run changed the hash of all of them, and two more generated folders did the same, `packages/core/native/` (the addon is built while the other suites already run, so the first and second `:test` hashed different sets) and `packages/visual/diffs/`; `plugins/shared` hashes the plugins' `node_modules` through its own `/plugins/*/**/*`. `.moon/tasks/all.yml` now excludes `node_modules`, `native/`, `diffs/` and their `/packages/` forms, and adds `/pnpm-lock.yaml`; the addon, which every suite loads under `WEFT_ENGINE=auto` once it is built, is instead a dependency of the inherited `test` task next to `root:wasm`, so it is built before any suite starts and its hash (its crate closure) flows into every suite's hash (`tooling:test` replaces its deps and is unaffected) (moon also hashes each project's own resolved dependencies, so a dependency change still invalidates); `plugins/shared/moon.yml` excludes `/plugins/**/node_modules/**`. Excluding the files was chosen over moving Vitest's `cacheDir`, which would fix only Vitest's cache and not the other generated folders. Demonstrated on the same tree: `moon run mcp:test` twice (the second cached, same hash); a one-line change in `packages/mcp/src/index.ts` re-ran it and reverting restored the first hash; then, with the addon as a dependency, `moon run :test` (4 m 52 s) followed by a second run of 2.8 s with every test task cached, `root:cli` being the one that runs, as it is declared `cache: false` (cargo's own up-to-date check, 2 s). A one-line change in `crates/weft-node/src/lib.rs` rebuilt `root:native` and re-ran all 20 test projects, and reverting it restored the cached hashes. Sources in `research.md` section 16.

### T56. Narrow the WebAssembly task's inputs

`root:wasm` listed `/crates/**/*` as its input, so any crate change rebuilt both WebAssembly modules (about 45 s), even a change to a crate the modules do not contain, such as `weft-swiftui` or `weft-cli`. Found in T54. Required: make the inputs exactly the crates the two modules are built from (`weft-wasm` with and without `web`, and their dependency closure from `cargo metadata`), plus the workspace `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and the files the build reads; a test compares the declared inputs with the crate closure and fails when a dependency is added without updating them; the same for `root:native` if it has the same coarse input. Done when a change in `weft-swiftui` no longer rebuilds the modules, a change in `weft-core` still does, and the full check exits 0.

Result: the root `moon.yml` has the file groups `rust-shared` (the root `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`, `weft-binding`, `weft-catalog`, `weft-core`, `weft-import` and `weft-web`, and the two files `weft-catalog` includes from `packages/catalog/`), `wasm-crate` (`weft-wasm`) and `native-crate` (`weft-node`); `root:wasm` uses the first two and `root:native` the first and last, plus its `packages/core/package.json`. Neither builds `weft-swiftui`, `weft-cli` or `weft-snapshots`. The old list also missed `packages/catalog/tokens/default.tokens.json`, which `weft-catalog` embeds, so a change to the default tokens served a stale module; the groups name it. `tooling/closure.ts` (`crateFolders` over `cargo metadata`, `externalIncludes` over the sources) and `tooling/test/inputs.test.ts` (11 tests: unit cases over a fixture workspace and the check of both tasks against the real workspace: exactly the closure's crate globs, `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` present, every include from outside a crate folder listed) fail when a dependency or an include is added without updating the groups; the `tooling` task's inputs gain the files the test reads. Removing `weft-web` or the tokens file from the groups made the test fail. `tooling/select.ts` lost its two `TASK_CRATE` entries for the build tasks, which the per-crate globs make dead, and `tooling/test/select.test.ts` gained two cases. Demonstrated: after a line appended to `crates/weft-swiftui/src/lib.rs` or `crates/weft-cli/src/main.rs`, `moon run root:wasm` was cached (7 ms, same hash); after a line in `crates/weft-core/src/lib.rs` it rebuilt (16 s); reverted, cached again; `root:native` was cached after a `weft-swiftui` change and rebuilt after a `weft-web` change. A change in a crate's `tests/` folder still rebuilds, because the folder is kept whole (a build script or a macro could read any file in it); `research.md` section 16 gives the reason. Sources and measurements are there too.

### T55. Default cross-axis alignment of a row stack

SPEC lists `align` (`start`/`center`/`end`/`stretch`) on `stack` but gives it no default, so the targets disagree. SwiftUI's `HStack` centres its children, while on the web a flex row stretches them: in todo-list, a button next to a field grows to the field's height (seen in T53).

Following SwiftUI and common design-system practice, the creator chose to define the default in SPEC: a `row` stack without `align` centres its children on the cross axis. A `column` stack keeps its current behaviour, and an explicit `align` always wins.

- SPEC §stack states both defaults.
- The base stylesheet, the static page and the generated components apply `center` to a row with no `align`.
- The reference renderer and the Figma/Penpot builds do the same.
- SwiftUI needs no change, but a test pins it.
- Importers do not write `align="center"` back for a row when it is the default.

Done when:
- the affected baselines and snapshots are retaken and reviewed;
- todo-list's button no longer stretches;
- the full check exits 0.

Result: SPEC §5.1 now says a `row` stack without `align` centres its children on the cross axis (as SwiftUI's `HStack` does), a `column` keeps its host's layout, and an explicit `align` wins. The static page's `.weft-row` rule, the generated React and SolidJS inline styles (including a `_align(value, direction)` runtime helper for a bound alignment or direction), the reference renderer and the Figma and Penpot builds all apply it; an `align` that is not one of the four values counts as absent. The DOM, HTML, React and SolidJS importers do not read a row's `center` back as `align`, so a source `align="center"` on a row reads back without it; the corpus's dashboard header and the stack example lost that now-redundant attribute, and `center` stays covered by a column stack. SwiftUI needed no change: a test pins that a row without `align` prints a plain `HStack` and reads back without an alignment, and that an explicit `align` still prints `alignment:`. Renderer and importer tests pin the default. The web and static page baselines of todo-list, dashboard, error-state and search-results, the Figma and Penpot layer trees and the HTML, React and SolidJS snapshots were retaken and reviewed: todo-list's button no longer stretches to the field.

### T50. Richer controls
The catalog has `select`, `menu`, `radio-group`, `checkbox` and `switch`, but none of the controls richer screens need. Add these kinds, as the creator chose:
- a slider (a range with min, max and step);
- a stepper (a number with a step);
- a date and time picker;
- a colour picker;
- a segmented control;
- a combobox. Say in the plan whether it is a new kind or a mode of `select`, and why.
For each kind:
- **Research first:** the WAI-ARIA pattern, the HTML element, the SwiftUI view, and the Figma/Penpot drawing, cited in `research.md`.
- **SPEC and catalog:** add them to SPEC (kinds, props, events and bindings) and to the catalog.
- **Every target:** the reference renderer, React, SolidJS, the static page, SwiftUI and the design tools.
- **Importers:** each importer that can read the kind back reads it.
Add one or more corpus screens that use every new kind, with data, and cover them wherever the existing corpus is covered:
- insta snapshots;
- Chromium screenshots of React, SolidJS and the static page;
- SwiftUI simulator screenshots;
- round trips;
- Figma and Penpot builds over the fakes.
The coverage test that every kind, prop and enum is used must still pass. Done when the full check exits 0 and new baselines are reviewed.

Result: six kinds in SPEC §5.1 and the catalog, each written from the research in `research.md` §15. `slider` (role `slider`: `value`, `min` 0, `max` 100, `step` 1), `stepper` (`spinbutton`: `value`, optional `min` and `max`, `step`), `date-picker` (`textbox`: `type` `date`/`time`/`datetime`, `value`, `min`, `max`, in `yyyy-mm-dd`, `hh:mm` and `yyyy-mm-ddThh:mm`), `color-picker` (`textbox`: `value` as `#rrggbb`), `segmented-control` (`radiogroup`) with its `segment` (`radio`), and `combobox` (`combobox`, with `option` content and a free-text `value`). The combobox is a new kind, not a `select` mode: a `select`'s value is always one of its options and a combobox's is the typed text, so one prop would mean two things and every target would carry two code paths behind one name. The number, date and colour rules (bad `step` is 1, `max` below `min` is `min`, an out-of-range value shows as the nearest bound, an invalid date shows as empty, a bad colour as black) live once in `weft-core::controls`; the TypeScript renderer, the JSX runtime and the Swift helpers mirror them. Targets: the reference renderer, React, SolidJS and the static page share one markup (native `input` types, a `data-weft-stepper` span with two pointer-only buttons, `data-weft-segmented` on a radio group, `input list` with a `datalist`); SwiftUI prints `Slider`, `Stepper`, `DatePicker` over a UTC string binding, `ColorPicker`, a segmented `Picker` and a `TextField` with a trailing `Menu`, through `weft…` helpers that keep a slider range from ever reversing, and a number bound to a slider or stepper is a `Double` in the model; Figma and Penpot draw the four leaf kinds in the component library and build the two container kinds as frames. Importers: the HTML, DOM, React and SolidJS importers read the markup hints above (and a slider or stepper value through the `_num` helper), the ARIA importer reads the roles, and the SwiftUI importer reads the printed constructs back exactly, including an explicit value against an absent one. An ARIA snapshot alone cannot tell a stepper, date or colour picker from a field, a combobox from a select, or a segmented control from a radio group, so the snapshot round trip of the two new screens is pinned as a known gap, and the React and SolidJS importers still lose the options of a bound `<each>` inside a `select` or `combobox` as they already did for `select`. Two corpus screens, `booking` (combobox over `<each>`, date, time and datetime pickers with bounds, stepper, slider, segmented control, colour picker, footer button) and `appearance` (disabled variants, bound and literal values, a bound segment label), bring the corpus to 19 screens and use every new kind, prop and enum, with insta snapshots, the Figma and Penpot layer trees, Chromium baselines for React, SolidJS and the static page, SwiftUI simulator baselines, and round trips. The base stylesheet got minimal token-driven rules for the new controls. The SwiftUI helper block grew, so every SwiftUI snapshot changed once.

### T59. Deterministic home indicator in SwiftUI screenshots

After T50 merged, one full check failed 21 SwiftUI screenshots by about 5,900 pixels each. On every screen the only difference was the iPhone home indicator at the bottom, which showed in that run but not in the baselines. A rerun of `visual:test` alone passed 277 of 277. The suite hides the status bar (`.statusBarHidden(true)` in the host app) but leaves the home indicator to the simulator's state.

- Make it deterministic. Prefer hiding it in the host app with the documented SwiftUI API (`persistentSystemOverlays(.hidden)` or the current equivalent, cited), if a screenshot then never shows it. Otherwise exclude the home-indicator strip from the comparison, using the device's safe-area inset rather than a hard-coded size.
- Keep both simulator modes (T46) working.

Done when:
- the suite passes repeatedly (at least three consecutive runs);
- baselines are retaken only if the visible area changes, and are then reviewed;
- the full check exits 0.

Result: the home indicator is drawn by the system inside the bottom safe-area inset, and an app cannot switch it off. `persistentSystemOverlays(.hidden)` (iOS 16 and later, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/view/persistentsystemoverlays(_:), checked 2026-10-05) is a preference the system "might or might not" honour: with it in the host app, the third of three consecutive runs still failed `appearance`, `booking`, `inbox` and `search-results` by 5,915 to 5,922 pixels each, all in one strip (x 387 to 818, y 2583 to 2597 of a 1206 by 2622 screenshot), so hiding was not used. Instead `compare` and `matchBaseline` take `ignoreBottom`, the number of pixel rows blanked in both images, and every SwiftUI comparison passes the height of the bottom safe-area inset. The host app reads that inset from its window (a view that ignores the safe area is told zero) and writes it to its container, and the test reads it with `simctl get_app_container` and scales it to the screenshot's width (34 pt, 102 px at 3x on the current device), so no size is written down. No baseline was retaken: the visible area is unchanged. Proof: the four captured failing frames matched their baselines with 0 differing pixels once masked; the SwiftUI suite (42 tests) then passed in three consecutive runs with `WEFT_SIMULATOR=own` and once with `WEFT_SIMULATOR=shared`; `test/compare.test.ts` pins that a difference in the ignored rows does not count and one above them does. `packages/visual/README.md` documents the rule.

### T58. Natural `align="center"` coverage in the corpus

T55 made `center` the default for a row, so T50 moved the corpus's explicit `center` onto the dashboard's `notices` column. The alerts there now sit centred at different widths, which looks odd.

- Restore the `notices` column to its earlier layout, without `align`.
- Cover `center` where centring is natural: a column in a coverage-only screen (dashboard, account, orders, inbox, leaderboard, booking or appearance), for example an empty-state message.
- Leave the twelve benchmark screens alone: they have HTML, JSX and A2UI versions and tasks in `corpus/tasks.json`.

Done when:
- the coverage test still sees `center`;
- the affected snapshots, screenshots and Figma/Penpot layer trees are retaken and reviewed;
- the full check exits 0.

Result: the dashboard's `notices` column has no `align` again, so its alerts stretch to the full width as they did before T50. `center` is now covered where centring is natural: the drafts list's empty slot in `corpus/inbox` is a column with `align="center"` holding the "No drafts." text and a new hint text. Inbox is a coverage-only screen, so no benchmark screen, HTML, JSX or A2UI version or task changed. The coverage test (which walks the corpus) still sees `center`. Retaken and reviewed: the insta snapshots of both screens in every format, the differential fixtures that carry the corpus (`crates/weft-core/tests/fixtures/differential.json`, `crates/weft-web/tests/fixtures/react.json` and `from-dom.json`), the Figma and Penpot layer trees (the `notices` frame is `start` again with no `align` in its source; the inbox gains the centred column), and the Chromium baselines of `dashboard` (React and the static page), checked downscaled: the three alerts now span the page width. The SwiftUI baselines did not change, since inbox shows its loading state in the screenshot and the dashboard's SwiftUI drawing is the same.

### T11. Streaming and incremental generation

A2UI and json-render use flat id lists so that a UI can render while a model is still writing it. Weft must show the same for nested markup: a truncated document parses into a renderable prefix with diagnostics only for the unfinished tail, and the renderer shows it. If it cannot, the trade-off is measured and written into `research.md`.

Execution plan:

1. Rust `weft-core` tokenizer: a `partial` mode. Input that ends inside a construct is not a syntax error but an unfinished tail: an unfinished start tag, end tag, comment or trailing reference is dropped, and each element still open at the end is kept. The diagnostics the end of input causes (W110 for open elements, the missing root) are returned as `pending`, not as errors, so the document is built.
2. `parse` takes `partial`: it builds the prefix document, validates it, and moves the checks a later chunk can still satisfy (W208 and W314 on an open element, W309 anywhere) from `diagnostics` to `pending`. `ParseResult` gains `pending`. The option travels in the existing options JSON, so both engines (wasm and native) need no new export.
3. `@weft/core`: `ParseOptions.partial`, `ParseResult.pending`.
4. Tests: Rust unit tests for each kind of cut; a vitest over every corpus screen truncated at every UTF-16 unit (valid prefix document, empty `diagnostics`, `pending` only for the tail, never a throw); a render-react test that renders partial documents.
5. SPEC section 6.3 "Streaming"; `research.md` section with the sources (A2UI, json-render) and the measured cost of re-parsing the growing text on every chunk.
6. Checks: `moon run root:changed` while iterating, the full check once at the end.

Result: partial parsing. `parse` takes `partial: true` (Rust `parse_partial`; the flag rides in the existing options JSON, so the wasm and native exports are unchanged) and `ParseResult` gains `pending`. Markup that stops anywhere reads as far as it goes: complete start tags and the text so far are kept, elements still open stay in the tree, and what the cut interrupts (start tag, end tag, comment, reference) is dropped. The diagnostics the cut causes (`W110`, `W114`, `W115`, plus `W208` and `W314` on an open element and `W309` anywhere, which a later chunk can still satisfy) go to `pending`; every other diagnostic stays in `diagnostics`, so a mistake in the finished part still blocks the document. The tokenizer's existing recovery (closing open elements at the end of the input) is reused, so complete parsing is unchanged. Tests: `crates/weft-core/tests/partial.rs` (each kind of cut, errors still reported, no panic), `crates/weft-catalog/tests/partial.rs` (the 19 corpus screens and the catalog examples cut after every character: valid prefix document, no error, only tail codes pending, the prefix never shrinks, the full text equals `parse`) and `packages/render-react/test/stream.test.ts` (every UTF-16 unit through both engines, rendered every 11th prefix, plus a partial document shown by the renderer). SPEC §6.3 documents the behaviour. `research.md` §17 records the sources (A2UI v0.9, json-render) and the measurement: re-parsing the whole text after every 16 characters costs 0.1 to 0.3 ms per chunk, so no incremental parser was built.

### T10. Host capabilities

SPEC §8 says a host advertises `{ weft, catalogs }` and an agent writes only what is advertised; no code does this. The MCP server reports its capabilities, loads catalogs, tokens and actions from configuration, and turns token and action checks on when they are given.

Result: the MCP server gained `weft_capabilities` (`packages/mcp/src/tools/capabilities.ts`), which answers `{ weft, catalogs: [{ name, version }] }` and, when the host checks them, `tokens`, `actions` (count and the first 200 sorted names, `truncated` beyond that) and `data`; an absent key means nothing is checked there. A project extension is listed after the core catalog it extends. It takes the same `project` argument as the other tools and fails with the project's diagnostics when it has errors. Loading catalogs, tokens and actions from configuration and turning the token and action checks on already existed (`weft-mcp --project`, `project` argument, T31) and is reused, not duplicated; a broken project still stops the server rather than being served half-loaded. The primer and server instructions point to the tool; SPEC §8, `docs/mcp.md`, the package README and `research.md` describe it; the plugin bundles were rebuilt.

### T18. Follow-ups from the prototype

- `fromDom` recovers slot membership from the renderer's `data-weft-slot` wrappers; SPEC §9 stops listing slots as always lost from DOM.
- Catalog fields for the validator rules that are still tied to specific kinds (`tabs.selected` names a `tab`, `screen` only at the root).
- Corpus: per-row accessible names for the Delete buttons in `data-table`; singular and plural in the `todo-list` counter.

Result: none of the three items was done before. (1) `from_dom` reads `data-weft-slot` wrappers on rendered HTML (a table's empty slot row loses its cell on the way); the always-on `slots` loss is gone from `DOM_LOSSES`, so only content on a page that does not mark its slots is reported, and `SPEC.md` §9, `docs/importing.md` and `docs/tour.md` say so; `<footer>` stays a slot of the generators' own pages only. (2) The catalog has `references` (a prop whose literal value is the id of an element of that kind) and `root` (the one kind a document root must be, and that stands nowhere else), set on `tabs.selected` and `screen` in the core catalog; `validate.rs` reads them with the same codes and messages (`W309`, `W201`, `W312`), the catalog diff rates them (naming a kind or becoming the root is major, dropping either is minor) and `SPEC.md` describes them. A catalog without a `root` kind no longer requires one. The importer's own `screen` checks in `weft-import` still name the kind. (3) The Delete buttons of `data-table` keep the visible text "Delete" and are named by the row (`label` bound to `$user.deleteLabel`, an `aria-label` on the web and an `accessibilityLabel` in SwiftUI, so the button stays short), and the `todo-list` counter is one `text` bound to `$.remainingLabel`, since Weft has no interpolation or plural rule and the host supplies the phrase; all four corpus renditions (HTML as `data-bind="label:…"`, JSX as a bound `aria-label`, A2UI as an `accessibility.label` path), the four bench adapters (a label names a text-content kind instead of its text), the two bench edit tasks, the insta snapshots, the fixtures and the web, static page and SwiftUI baselines were updated.

### T51. Glass material tokens
A frosted-glass surface is expressed as a token, as the creator chose: a `material` token (background blur, tint colour and opacity) that an element takes through its style, like other tokens.
- **Research:** the DTCG 2025.10 format has no material type, so the plan cites the format, says how the token is written (a Weft extension type or a composite of DTCG types), and keeps standard tools able to read the file.
- **Web targets:** `backdrop-filter` with a solid fallback where it is unsupported.
- **SwiftUI:** `glassEffect` on iOS 26 and later (or the current Liquid Glass API, cited), `Material` before it.
- **Figma and Penpot:** a background blur with the tint fill, read back on export.
- **Modes:** dark and light modes (T45) may give the material different values.
Add a corpus screen with glass surfaces over an image or gradient background, so the effect is visible in screenshots. Cover it like the corpus: snapshots, Chromium, simulator, and Figma/Penpot fakes. Done when the full check exits 0 and new baselines are reviewed.

Result: a frosted-glass surface is a `material` token, a DTCG `color` token (the tint, its `alpha` the opacity) with the vendor extension `dev.weft.material` holding the `blur` dimension, so other DTCG tools read an ordinary colour and keep the blur (research.md §17, SPEC §10.3). The loader gives it the kind `material` and bounds it (alpha 0 to 1, blur 0 to 100 px) with the new `T007` (`W705` in a project); the default tokens and the example project (with a dark override) carry `material.glass`. A `stack` or `grid` takes it through the token-typed prop `material` (`material="{token.material.glass}"`), checked by `W306` and `W307` as any token. Web: the token is `--weft-<path>-tint`, `-solid` and `-blur`, and the element sets private `--_weft-material-*` properties and `data-weft-material`; the base stylesheet paints the solid colour and adds `backdrop-filter` inside `@supports` (static page, React, SolidJS, reference renderer, importers). SwiftUI: a nested `Surface` modifier, `glassEffect` on iOS 26 and later (Swift 6.2), tint over a system `Material` before it; the tint follows the appearance, the single blur radius only picks the material's thickness. Figma: a tint fill plus a `BACKGROUND_BLUR` effect; Penpot: the same fill plus `backgroundBlur`; both keep the prop in plugin data and fingerprint the blur. The `glass` corpus screen (21 screens now) has snapshots for every generator, Chromium baselines for the static page and React, SwiftUI baselines in light and dark, and the example project's review lines are glass in both appearances, all over a striped backdrop and reviewed.

### T52. 3D transforms

Split from the original "3D transforms and models" by the task size rule (500 lines of code, 1000 of tests): the model element is T52.1.

**3D transforms on any element:** rotation on each axis, perspective and depth, e.g. a flipped or tilted card.
- Web: CSS `perspective` and `rotate3d` (as the `transform` list).
- SwiftUI: `rotation3DEffect`.
- Figma and Penpot: the nearest 2D projection, with a note when it is lossy.
- Importers read the transforms back.

Add a corpus screen with tilted cards, covered like the corpus: snapshots, Chromium, simulator, and Figma/Penpot fakes. Screenshots must be deterministic: a fixed transform, no animation. Done when the full check exits 0 and new baselines are reviewed.

**Decisions (research in `research.md`, section 18):**
- **Four universal attributes** (SPEC section 2.2, so they work on any element): `rotate-x`, `rotate-y`, `rotate-z` (degrees, -360 to 360) and `perspective` (the viewer's distance in px, at least 1; absent means no perspective). All four are literals (`bindable: false`). Web draws `transform: perspective(p) rotateX(x) rotateY(y) rotateZ(z)`; SwiftUI draws `rotation3DEffect` (z first, then y, then x, so both compose the same matrix). Depth is the perspective distance; a separate z translation is not added (no SwiftUI form without `projectionEffect`).
- **Design tools draw only the turn in the picture plane:** `rotate-z` becomes the layer's rotation (Figma counts counterclockwise, so its sign flips); `rotate-x`, `rotate-y` and `perspective` are not drawn, because a cosine scale would break auto layout sizes. The exact values stay in the layer's Weft source, so a round trip returns them. Turning a layer by hand is a `tokens` visual-edit loss, through the style fingerprint (no new loss on an unedited read, which the round trip tests require).

Result: any element takes four literal attributes (SPEC section 2.2): `rotate-x`, `rotate-y`, `rotate-z` (degrees, -360 to 360) and `perspective` (px, at least 1), none of them bindable. The static page, React and SolidJS components and the reference renderer write one `transform: perspective(p) rotateX(x) rotateY(y) rotateZ(z)` next to the layout style, in the same string (`weft-web/src/tilt.rs`, `render-react/src/values.ts`); a dialog is the exception, since it is drawn into the top layer. The HTML, DOM and JSX importers read the transform back (strict grammar, each function once), and any other `transform` is still a layout loss. SwiftUI prints a `rotation3DEffect` chain, z then y then x, with the perspective on the last one only as `400 / px` (SwiftUI's perspective is relative to the view, so this is exact only for a view about 400 points across; the importer reads the pixels back). A dialog keeps its tilt as a `weftProp` marker. The Swift importer gained a tuple expression to read the axis. Figma and Penpot draw only `rotate-z`, as the layer's rotation (Figma counts counterclockwise), because an x or y tilt as a cosine scale would break auto layout sizes; the exact values stay in the layer's Weft source, and turning a layer by hand is a `tokens` visual-edit loss through the style fingerprint, which only gains a value when the layer is turned, so no existing snapshot changed. The new corpus screen `tilt` (21 screens now) has differential fixtures, insta snapshots, layer snapshots and Chromium, static page and simulator baselines, reviewed by eye; the web and SwiftUI turns go the same way. The model element is T52.1. Open: the Penpot rotation direction is not stated in `@penpot/plugin-types` 1.5.0 and was taken as clockwise (unverified against a real file).

### T60. Deterministic top strip and blur in SwiftUI screenshots

After T59 masked the home indicator, two more SwiftUI screenshot flakes showed up on main in four full visual runs: `tilt` once differed by 38,537 px because the capture included the black Dynamic Island pill at the top, and `glass-dark` twice differed by 2 px, most likely from blur noise in the glass effect. Both pass on rerun, so the merge gate is unreliable.

Mask the top system strip the same way T59 masks the bottom one (the host app measures the top safe-area inset from its window and the comparison blanks it in both images), or keep the island out of the capture if the simulator allows it. For the glass screens, prefer a deterministic render; if blur stays noisy, allow a small, documented per-screen pixel tolerance for those screens only. Retake baselines only if the compared area changes, and review them. Done when the SwiftUI suite passes 5 consecutive runs in `WEFT_SIMULATOR=own` and 1 in `shared`, and the full check exits 0.

Result: the top strip is masked like the bottom one. `tilt` failed because some simulator screenshots include the Dynamic Island and the screen's rounded corners (38,537 pixels in x 0 to 791, y 0 to 152 of 1206 by 2622), which the baselines lack. The host app now writes the window's top safe-area inset (62 pt, 186 px; the island ends at 152 px, and the inset is nonzero even with the status bar hidden) to `insets.json` beside the bottom one, `compare` and `matchBaseline` take `ignoreTop` beside `ignoreBottom`, and every SwiftUI comparison passes both. The `glass-dark` failure was 372 pixels differing by small amounts, all at the glass corners, of which pixelmatch counted 2 (the largest channel delta was 43). The system draws the glass, so its blur cannot be made deterministic from the app, and it was not reproducible when the machine was quiet: 12 launches each of `glass`, `glass-dark` and `tilt` matched their baselines exactly and one another. `glass` and `glass-dark`, and only those, therefore accept up to 16 differing pixels (`tolerance` of `compare`; `GLASS_TOLERANCE` in `test/swiftui.test.ts`; documented in `packages/visual/README.md`). No baseline was retaken: the compared area only shrank. Proof: `test/compare.test.ts` pins the top strip and the tolerance; the SwiftUI suite passed in five consecutive runs with `WEFT_SIMULATOR=own` and once with `shared`.

### T15. Lit web components target

A generator for Lit web components, to show that the format is not bound to React (the importer from a Custom Elements Manifest is T15.1). Done when a corpus screen renders through the Lit target with the same accessibility tree as the React renderer.

Result: `Framework::Lit` joins React and SolidJS in `crates/weft-web/src/jsx`, so it shares their render plan; `jsx/lit.rs` prints the element tree as `html` templates (whitespace-exact: line breaks only inside tags) in a `LitElement` subclass that renders into the light DOM, takes `data`, `actions` and `onChange` as properties and registers a custom element (`weft-<name>` when the component name has no hyphen). Events become `@event`, booleans `?attr`, `value` and `checked` `.prop`, styles `styleMap`, and dynamic tags use `unsafeStatic` from a closed set. TypeScript output and the `source` comment are refused for Lit (`JsxError`). Surfaces: `weft lit`, `export.lit.outDir`, `framework: "lit"` in `@weft/to-jsx`, the `lit` wire framework. Tests: 57 insta snapshots (`lit-js`), a binding test, 61 `@weft/to-jsx` tests (module shape, hostile strings stay values, no whitespace between tags), and in `@weft/visual` the Chromium accessibility tree of the Lit element equals React's on all 21 corpus screens, plus two behaviour tests (action click, input event with `onChange`). `lit` 3.3.3 is a pinned devDependency of `@weft/visual` (registry.npmjs.org). Docs: SPEC section 9 and 10, `docs/cli.md`, `docs/projects.md`.

### T52.1. 3D models

Split from T52. A `model` element showing a 3D asset, with a still fallback image and an accessible label.

- **A new leaf kind** `model` (role `img`, label required) with `src` (glTF, `.glb` or `.gltf`), `usdz` (optional, for Apple platforms) and `fallback` (a still image, required). glTF is what `<model-viewer>` loads and USDZ is what RealityKit loads, and neither reads the other's format, so the element names both.
- **Web: `<model-viewer>`** (`@google/model-viewer`, Apache-2.0, 4.3.1 on npm). Markup `<model-viewer src alt>` with the fallback `<img slot="poster">` as its child, so a page without the script, and every screenshot, shows the fallback. The host page loads the script; Weft never injects one.
- **SwiftUI:** `Model3D` is visionOS only (the SDK marks it `iOS unavailable`), so a helper uses `Model3D` on visionOS, `RealityView` with the bundled USDZ on iOS 18 and macOS 15, and the fallback `AsyncImage` elsewhere and while loading. An environment value forces the fallback, and the screenshot host sets it so images are deterministic.
- **Asset paths are untrusted:** the three paths are literals validated by a new code `W317` (relative path without `..`, backslash, control characters or a scheme, or an `https` URL; the right extension; at most 2048 bytes), and renderers apply the existing `safe_url` / `safeUrl` guard again. One small properly licensed asset ships in the corpus with its licence noted; a test bounds its size. Design tools draw the library's image rectangle.
- Groundwork tried in T52 and removed again: the `model` kind in `packages/catalog/src/core.ts`, `asset_problem` in `weft-core/src/rules.rs`, the `W317` check in `validate.rs`, cases in `packages/core/test/cases.ts` and `crates/weft-core/tests/codes.rs`, `model` in the fixture catalog `packages/core/test/catalog.ts`.
- Add a corpus screen with a model, covered like the corpus. Screenshots are deterministic (the fallback). Done when the full check exits 0 and new baselines are reviewed.

Execution plan:

1. Core: the `model` kind in `packages/catalog/src/core.ts` (props `bindable: false`, regenerate `catalog.json`), `asset_problem` in `rules.rs`, `W317` in `validate.rs`, `diagnostics.rs` and `diagnostics.ts`, cases in `codes.rs`, `cases.ts` and the fixture catalog, SPEC §5.1 and §6.2, `AGENT-SPEC.md`.
2. Web: the static page and the React and SolidJS generators write `<model-viewer>` with the poster `<img>` child (`html.rs`, `jsx/mod.rs`), the DOM importers read it back (`dom.rs`), the reference renderer does the same (`render-react`), all through the existing `safe_url` / `safeUrl` guard.
3. SwiftUI: a `WeftModel` helper, added to a file only when the screen has a model (`Model3D` on visionOS, `RealityView` with the USDZ elsewhere, the fallback image while loading and when the process sets `WEFT_STILL_MODELS`); the importer reads the call back.
4. Design tools: the library draws the fallback image rectangle (`packages/design-tool`, Figma and Penpot snapshots).
5. Corpus: a `models` screen and a small generated asset (glTF binary, USDZ, still PNG) in `corpus/models/assets/` under the project licence, a size-bound test; regenerate the differential and importer fixtures and snapshots; screenshots show the fallback.
6. Verify with `moon run root:changed` while iterating, then the full check; close the task.

Result: `model` is a leaf kind (role `img`, label required) with literal `src` (`.glb` or `.gltf`), `usdz` (optional) and `fallback` (a still image), checked by the new `W317` through `asset_problem` in `weft-core/src/rules.rs`; the three renderers apply the same rule again (`model_asset_url` in `weft-web`, `modelAssetUrl` in `@weft/render-react`, which a test compares with the core over 28 sample paths). The static page and the React and SolidJS generators write `<model-viewer role="img" alt src ios-src camera-controls interaction-prompt="none">` with a `<img slot="poster">` child, never a script; the importers read it back (`<model-viewer>` is named in `dom.rs`, so a bare `img` stays an `image`). SwiftUI gets a `WeftModel` helper only in a file whose screen has a model: `Model3D` on visionOS, `RealityView` with the bundled USDZ on iOS 18 and macOS 15, the still elsewhere, while loading, and in a process that sets `WEFT_STILL_MODELS`, which the screenshot host sets; the importer reads the call back, with `src` kept as a `weftProp` marker. Design tools draw a grey 160 by 120 rectangle. The corpus screen `showroom` has two models (one tilted) and an octahedron that `corpus/showroom/assets/generate.ts` writes (`gem.glb` 1.5 KiB, `gem.usdz` 0.9 KiB, `gem.png` 2.4 KiB), so it carries no third-party licence (Apache-2.0 like the repository; `usdchecker` accepts the USDZ); `bench/test/model-assets.test.ts` bounds the sizes and compares the glb and usdz with the generator byte for byte. Screenshots (Chromium and the iOS Simulator) show the still: a live `model-viewer` or `RealityView` has no fixed first frame in CI, and the visual suite serves `gem.png` from the corpus. The live `@google/model-viewer` script and the RealityKit path are not run by any test. SPEC section 5.1, 6, 9 and AGENT-SPEC.md, the differential and importer fixtures, the Figma and Penpot layer snapshots and the insta snapshots are updated. No new dependency.

### T61. Valid change handler for a read-only bound control

The JSX generator can print `onChange={(_e) => const _v = ...}` for a number, date, colour or combobox control whose `value` is bound but not writable and that has no `on-change`: `Self::handler` prints a lone statement as an arrow body, and a `const` declaration is not an expression. The fast-check property "any input yields a well-formed module" in `packages/to-jsx/test/generate.test.ts` fails on some seeds (for example 502940636). Fix it in `valued` (`crates/weft-web/src/jsx/mod.rs`) or `handler`, add a deterministic regression test for React, SolidJS and Lit, and keep generated output unchanged where it was valid. Done when the regression test and the property test with that seed pass and the full check exits 0.

Result: `valued` now leaves out the lone `const _v = ...` read when the bound `value` has neither a write-back nor an `on-change`, so the handler prints as `(_e) => {}` (kept, because React warns about a controlled input without one). Output with a write-back or a change event is unchanged: no snapshot changed. Test: `a read-only bound control keeps a valid change handler on every target` in `packages/to-jsx/test/generate.test.ts` (slider, stepper, date, colour, combobox on React, SolidJS and Lit); fast-check seed 502940636 of the well-formed-module property passes. The plugin `wasm-web` bundles were rebuilt.

### T13. Interoperability with A2UI

Export a Weft document to A2UI v0.9 messages and import A2UI messages back, with a loss table in SPEC §9. The json-render half was split off as T13.1 when T13 was claimed, because both directions of both formats do not fit the 500-line limit. Done when every corpus screen converts both ways and the losses are listed.

Result: new crate `weft-interop` (`a2ui::to_a2ui`, `a2ui::from_a2ui`; sources: `a2ui-project/a2ui` at `4787774`, 2026-10-05, `specification/v0_9`). The exporter writes `createSurface` and `updateComponents` over the basic catalog (the stand-in table of `corpus/README.md` is its mapping) and records every loss; the importer reads a JSON array, one object or JSON Lines into the `weft-import` role tree, so ids, required props and validation come from the shared builder, and an `<each>` comes back from a template. `weft a2ui` and `weft import-a2ui` print losses on stderr; `export.a2ui.outDir` and `import.a2ui.outDir` are in SPEC §10.6, `weft.schema.json` and `docs/`. SPEC §9 has "To A2UI", "From A2UI" and a loss table for both. Tests (`weft-snapshots`, `tests/interop.rs`): every corpus screen and catalog example exports to messages that pass the vendored A2UI v0.9 JSON Schemas (`jsonschema` 0.58.5, dev dependency), imports back to a document with no errors in lenient validation, and reaches a fixed point on the second trip; the 12 hand-made `screen.a2ui.json` renditions import; insta snapshots pin the messages, the documents and the losses of both directions. Size exception: the code is about 1,500 lines (export 790, import 630) against the 500-line limit, approved by the creator because export and import share one mapping, so they were not split further.

### T15.1. Catalog import from a Custom Elements Manifest

An importer that turns a Custom Elements Manifest (schema 2.1.0, https://github.com/webcomponents/custom-elements-manifest) into a Weft catalog: each custom element becomes a kind, its attributes and fields become props, its slots become slots, its events become events. Done when the manifest of a real component library imports into a catalog that validates, with the lost parts listed.

Result: `import_cem` in `crates/weft-import/src/cem.rs` turns the text of a Custom Elements Manifest (schema 2.x, `custom-elements-manifest` 2.1.0, npm, published 2024-05-06, BSD-3-Clause; `schema.json` and `schema.d.ts` read from the package) into a catalog extension that the project loader of SPEC section 10.4 accepts, plus a loss list and diagnostics; it reuses `Loss`, `LossKind`, `squash`, `clean`, `parse_json` and the catalog model, and adds no second catalog format. Kinds come from `tagName` or a `custom-element-definition` export; attributes and public writable fields merge into props (boolean, number, string, enum of string literals, defaults only when a literal of the type); slots and events carry over, camelCase names become kebab-case, the role is `generic` (a manifest has none). Input is bounded (10 MB, 2000 elements, 1000 members per list: `W602`), unreadable input or another schema version is `W601`, and names, enum values, defaults and descriptions are checked against the Weft grammars, a property test over arbitrary names, types and defaults checking that the catalog always loads clean. Tests (`crates/weft-import/tests/cem.rs`, 9 tests, fixture `fixtures/cem/acme-ui.json` in the shape of a Lit library manifest): the kinds, props, slots and events, every loss, the catalog loading as a project extension without diagnostics and then validating documents (`W203`, `W204`, `W206`, `W207`, `W402` for misuse), hostile input and the limits. SPEC section 9 has the mapping and the losses. Size: about 580 lines of code against the 500-line limit; the command line and bindings were split off as T15.2, and the importer could not be cut further without dropping features. Not checked: a manifest of a real published library (none was in the repository and none was downloaded).

### T62. Native addon as a dependency of the WebAssembly runtime legs

`packages/core/test/engines.test.ts` loads the native addon on every leg (`loadNative({ choice: "native" })`) and compares its surface with the WebAssembly module whenever an addon is present. `root:runtimes-node` and `root:runtimes-bun` depend only on `root:wasm`, so after a merge that changes the Rust core they can run against a stale addon and fail until `moon run root:native` is run by hand (seen twice, in T52.1 and T15.1). Add `root:native` to their deps, as T57 did for the test suites. Done when both legs depend on it and the full check exits 0.

Result: `root:runtimes-node` and `root:runtimes-bun` now depend on `root:native` as well as `root:wasm`, with a comment in `moon.yml` saying why. `moon run root:runtimes tooling:test root:lint` passes (411 tests on each leg).

### T67. Weft to Slint and back

Convert a Weft screen into a Slint component (`.slint`, Slint 1.x with `std-widgets.slint`) and read Slint source back into Weft, so a Weft screen can drive a native desktop app (macOS now, Windows from the same code, the web later through Slint's WebAssembly build) and a Slint UI can come back to Weft. A new crate, `crates/weft-slint`, follows `crates/weft-swiftui`: a pure-Rust generator plus an importer, developed on the branch `feat/weft-slint-convert`. The task is split by size: T67 is the library for the core vocabulary in both directions with its tests, T67.1 the CLI and project settings, T67.2 the reader of hand-written Slint and the rest of the catalog.

**Weft side (input of the generator, output of the importer).** A strictly valid document (SPEC §2–§6) against the core catalog and the project tokens: the `<screen>` root with `weft`, elements with document-unique ids, props as literals, bindings (`{$.path}`, `{!$.path}`) or token references (`{token.path}`), events as `on-<event>` action names, named slots, and canonical markup and JSON (SPEC §3) as the form the importer returns.

**Slint side (output of the generator, input of the importer).** One file per screen:

| Part | Slint |
| --- | --- |
| Source comment | A leading `// weft:source slint [name=<name>]` line, then the canonical markup, one markup line per `// ` comment line |
| Imports | `import { … } from "std-widgets.slint";` with only the widgets the screen uses |
| Component | `export component <Name>Screen inherits Window`; `<Name>` is the PascalCase screen id or the `name` option; `title` from the screen's `label` |
| Data | One `in-out property <string\|bool\|float\|int>` per root data path a binding reads: `$.email` → `email`, `$.user.email` → `user-email` |
| Actions | One `callback perform(string, string)` that every event calls with the action name and the element id, like the SwiftUI target's `perform` handler |
| Body | A `VerticalBox` (`alignment: LayoutAlignment.start`) whose element id is the screen id, holding the elements |

**Kinds (Weft → Slint; the importer reads the same table back):**

| Weft | Slint |
| --- | --- |
| `screen` | the `Window` component and its `VerticalBox` |
| `stack` | `VerticalLayout` / `HorizontalLayout` (`direction`); a `gap` token → `spacing` in px (`rem` × 16) |
| `grid` | `GridLayout`; child `n` gets `row: n / columns` and `col: n % columns` (`columns` must be a literal) |
| `section` | `GroupBox` (`title` from `label`) around a `VerticalBox`; the `header` slot first |
| `form` | `VerticalBox`; the `footer` slot in a `HorizontalBox` after the content |
| `heading` | `Text` with the browser default size for `level` (32, 24, 19, 16, 13, 11 px) and weight 700 |
| `text` | `Text` |
| `link` | `TouchArea` around a `Text` in `Palette.accent-background`; `on-press` → `clicked` |
| `button` | `Button`; `primary: true` for `variant="primary"`; `on-press` → `clicked` |
| `field` | a caption `Text` above a `LineEdit` (`input-type: InputType.password` / `InputType.number`, `placeholder-text`); `type="multiline"` → `TextEdit` |
| `checkbox`, `switch` | `CheckBox`, `Switch`; `text` from `label` |
| `select` + `option` | a caption `Text` above a `ComboBox`: the option texts are its `model`; `value` picks `current-index` through a generated chain of comparisons, and `selected` writes the chosen option's `value` back |
| `slider` | a caption `Text` above a `Slider` (`float`): `minimum`, `maximum`, `value` |
| `stepper` | a caption `Text` above a `SpinBox` (`int`) |

**Props, states, ids, slots, bindings and actions:**

- **Ids.** A Weft id is the Slint element id (`email := LineEdit`). Slint reads `-` and `_` in an identifier as the same character and reserves `root`, `self` and `parent`, and an element id shadows the globals and enums the generated code names (`Palette`, `InputType`, `LayoutAlignment`), so a screen whose ids clash under these rules is refused with the element path.
- **Bindings.** A data property is typed from the props that read it: the type of the writable prop that writes it, otherwise `string`, `float`, `int` or `bool` in that order of precedence. A writable prop bound to a path is a two-way binding (`text <=> root.email`); any other binding is an expression; a boolean read of text is `root.x != ""` and of a number `root.x != 0` (SPEC §2.1 truthiness); `not` is negation. `disabled` becomes `enabled` and `hidden` becomes `visible`, each the binding read negated so no double `!` appears. Array-index and loop-variable paths, paths named like a `Window` property (`$.title`, `$.width`, …) and paths whose property names clash are refused with the binding path.
- **Actions.** `on-press` → `clicked`, `on-change` → `edited` (fields, `SpinBox`), `toggled` (`CheckBox`, `Switch`), `selected` (`ComboBox`) or `changed` (`Slider`); `on-submit` of a form runs from the `clicked` of its `submit="true"` buttons (after their own `on-press`) and from Enter in its `LineEdit`s (`accepted`). Every call is `root.perform("<action>", "<id>")`.
- **Slots.** `header` of `section` and `footer` of `form` are placed as SPEC §5.1 places them; other slots belong to kinds T67 does not map.
- **States and Weft-only props.** `state`, `required`, `error`, the field types `email` and `search`, `variant="danger"`, `tone`, `stack.align` and `wrap`, `step`, `href`, `material` and the 3D tilt have no Slint property: they are not rendered and survive only in the source comment. `label` on `button` and `heading` is `accessible-label`.
- **Trust.** Document text reaches the output only as Slint string literals in which every backslash is doubled, quotes are escaped and control and line-separator characters are `\u{…}`, so no `\{…}` interpolation or line break can form; ids reach it only after the identifier check.

**Slint → Weft.** The importer reads the document from the source comment and accepts it only when generating from it reproduces the file (lines compared trimmed, blank lines ignored); an edited file, a file generated with other tokens, a file without the comment, a comment that does not parse and input over 2 MB are refused with distinct errors. Reading hand-written or edited Slint with a loss table is T67.2.

**Lossy points.** Weft → Slint: the Weft-only props and states above, and token references, which become their px values (the comment keeps them). Slint → Weft (T67.2): expressions that are not a property read, handlers that do more than one `perform` call, styling (colours, fonts, sizes, padding), components from other files, `for` and `if` over anything but a property, and animations become losses in the shape of the other importers.

**Not in T67** (refused as `Unsupported` with the path until T67.2): `<each>`, `list`, `table`, `tabs`, `dialog`, `menu`, `image`, `model`, `alert`, the date and colour pickers, `radio-group`, `segmented-control`, `combobox` and catalog-extension kinds.

**Mandatory tests:**

- Unit tests, Weft → Slint: data-property typing and truthiness reads, refusal of clashing paths and ids, component naming, string-literal escaping.
- Unit and integration tests, Slint → Weft: read-back of every generated golden file, a reindented file, and refusal of an edited file, a tampered comment, a file without the comment, a broken comment and oversize input.
- Format tests: every generated file matches its reviewed golden file under `crates/weft-slint/tests/fixtures/` (`WEFT_UPDATE_FIXTURES=1` rewrites them) and compiles with `slint-interpreter` without errors; the read-back document passes the Weft validator in strict mode and serializes byte-identical to the canonical input (round trip Weft → Slint → Weft) for the `login`, `signup` and `settings` corpus screens and a fixture with the other mapped controls; document text with quotes, backslashes and `\{` compiles to exactly one component.
- `crates/weft-cli/tests/deps.rs`: `weft-slint` has no Slint crate among its normal dependencies.

Execution plan:

1. `crates/weft-slint` (workspace member, `publish = false`; normal dependencies `weft-core`, `weft-catalog`, `indexmap`, `thiserror`; `slint-interpreter` 1.18 as a dev-dependency only, without backends or renderers). Modules: `names` (identifiers, component names, string literals), `data` (property inference and reads), `generate`, `import`.
2. `generate(document, &GenerateOptions { catalog, tokens, name })` → `Result<String, GenerateError>`: strict validation first, then the id and data checks, then the component; `GenerateError::Unsupported` lists every refused element or binding path.
3. `import_slint(source, &ImportOptions { catalog, tokens })` → `Result<Document, ImportError>` from the source comment, verified by regeneration.
4. Tests as listed above: unit tests in `names.rs` and `data.rs`, `tests/slint.rs` with the golden files, the compile check, the round trip and the refusals; the assertion in `crates/weft-cli/tests/deps.rs`.
5. `SPEC.md` §9 "To Slint" and "From Slint" (additive), the crate `README.md`, the `slint-interpreter` row in `toolchain.md` (`rust.md` already lists it).
6. Verify with `cargo nextest run -p weft-slint -p weft-cli`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Done when all of the above pass under `cargo nextest run`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `SPEC.md` §9 has the "To Slint" and "From Slint" rules.

Result: `crates/weft-slint` with `generate` and `import_slint`; golden files for `login`, `signup`, `settings` and `tests/fixtures/controls.weft`, each compiled with `slint-interpreter` 1.18.1 and read back byte-identical to the canonical markup and strictly valid. `cargo nextest run -p weft-slint -p weft-cli` (57 tests, 15 of them in weft-slint), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` pass. The non-test code is about 830 lines, above the 500-line task size; it was kept in one task because the generator, the data typing and the read-back are tested only together.

### T70. weft validate --no-project on canonical JSON skipped the shape layer

`crates/weft-cli/src/main.rs` answered `vec![]` when no catalog/project applied to a `.json` input, so `weft validate --no-project broken.json` exited 0 with only a stderr hint — while the same document through the library, the MCP server or the markup path got its W200 shape diagnostics, and the hint claimed "only the syntax layer was checked". Found by the 2026-10-07 audit. Fix: the `None` branch runs the library `validate` with `catalog: None` (the shape layer runs before the catalog is consulted), and the hint now says "syntax and shape layers". Audit tasks T71–T73 recorded in plan.md (renumbered from the audit draft: T63–T66 were already taken in `done.md`).
Model: ZCode / GLM-5.3 · Status: done 2026-10-07 · Priority: P2 · Complexity: 1 · Files: `crates/weft-cli/src/main.rs`, `crates/weft-cli/tests/project.rs`
Check: `cargo test -p weft-cli` — 43 passed (new: `canonical_json_without_a_project_still_gets_its_shape_checked`); `cargo clippy -p weft-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean.

### T71. The Rust CLI no longer overwrites generated files without --force

`crates/weft-cli/src/convert.rs`'s shared `emit` `std::fs::write`-d over an existing target unconditionally, while the plugin scripts refuse to overwrite without `--force` — a behavior `docs/exporting-jsx.md` documents as a feature — so a hand-edited `gen/Login.jsx` was silently clobbered. Found by the 2026-10-07 audit. ### T67.4. Slint reader for the widgets the generator now emits

`read_slint` maps `for` to `each`, `ListView` to `list`, `StandardTableView` to `table`, `TabWidget` to `tabs`, `PopupWindow` to `dialog`, `MenuBar` and `Menu` to `menu`, `Image` to `image` (or `model` when the source comment says so), and `RadioGroup` to `radio-group` or `segmented-control`. A source comment that names an id `alert` or `combobox` reads that `VerticalLayout` or `ComboBox` as that kind; without the comment they stay a `stack` and a `select`. A generated screen with the source comment removed keeps those kinds and ids, and an unknown element stays a `kinds` loss whose children are still read. SPEC §9 "From Slint" lists the mapping. The reader is past the 500-line cap; the widget match and the expression reader are tested only together.

### T67.5. Slint reader recovers bindings and events

`read_expr.rs` reads the generator's expressions back: a `root` property (`user-email` is `$.user.email`, including `<=>`), truthiness (`!= ""`, `!= 0`), `enabled` and `visible` as `disabled` and `hidden`, and `root.perform` as the element's event. A caption `Text` folds into the control's label, a bold `Text` at the generator's font sizes is a heading level, a `ComboBox` model of string literals is `option` children, and a `spacing` in px that equals exactly one dimension token is that token. Without the source comment, `login`, `signup` and `settings` keep their bindings and actions; a `16px` spacing stays unmatched because two dimension tokens share it. SPEC §9 "From Slint" keeps a loss only for what this recovery does not read. `read.rs` and `read_expr.rs` together are past the 500-line cap and are tested only together.
Check: `cargo nextest run -p weft-slint --features import` — 30 passed, including `login_without_the_source_comment_keeps_bindings_and_submit` and `signup_and_settings_without_the_source_comment_keep_bindings`.

### T105.12. WinUI read-back: nesting bound

`import_winui` parses untrusted XAML with `roxmltree`, which recurses once per nested element, so a deeply nested source overflows the stack and aborts the process instead of returning an `ImportError`. Bound it as `read_winui` does: the `nested_past` pre-scan with `MAX_XML_DEPTH`, and the parse on `weft_import::on_reader_stack`. Done when a source nested past the bound is rejected with an error, not a crash.

**Execution plan.** (1) Move `nested_past` and `MAX_XML_DEPTH` from `crates/weft-winui/src/read/mod.rs` into `src/import.rs`, next to `MAX_XML_NODES`, so both readers share them; the public `MAX_XML_DEPTH` stays exported. (2) `import_winui` rejects a source nested past the bound as `ImportError::Xml` and runs the rest on `on_reader_stack`. (3) A test in `tests/read_back.rs` that fails with a stack overflow before the fix. (4) SPEC §9 "From WinUI": the nesting bound holds for `import_winui` too. Verify with `moon run root:changed -- --base origin/main`.

Result: `nested_past` and `MAX_XML_DEPTH` moved from `read/mod.rs` into `import.rs`, so `MAX_XML_DEPTH` is now exported without the `import` feature. `import_winui` returns `ImportError::Xml` for a source nested past 800 levels before parsing, and parses and compares on `weft_import::on_reader_stack`; a reader that panics there is `ImportError::Xml` too. SPEC §9 "From WinUI" states the bound once for both importers, as does `docs/winui-design.md`. Test: `deep_nesting_is_an_error_not_a_crash` in `crates/weft-winui/tests/read_back.rs` (a golden nested 8 levels under the bound is `Edited`, at the bound `Xml`), which aborted with a stack overflow before the fix.

### T105.9. Hand-written WinUI importer: bindings, events, tokens and layout

`{x:Bind ViewModel.…}` paths, the `Weft` function bindings and modes into bindings; `weft:On.*`, native `Command` and event bindings into actions; `Weft.*` resource references into tokens; `Grid` tracks back into `justify` and `grow`, alignments into `align`. About 450 lines of code. Done when every corpus screen reads back from its XAML alone with only the losses SPEC §9 lists.

**Execution plan.**

1. `crates/weft-winui/src/read/values.rs`: parse an `{x:Bind …}` value (path from `ViewModel.`/`WeftRoot.` with `[i]` indexes into `$.a.0.b`; `Mode=OneWay|TwoWay`, `UpdateSourceTrigger`; the function bindings `Weft.Not`, `Truthy`, `Hide` through a prefix that resolves to a `using:` namespace); apply it per conversion (`IsEnabled` and `Visibility` invert, markers keep `Not` as `{!…}`); a bound `Text`/`Content` is the `text` prop. A missing or `OneTime` mode, other options, `{Binding}` and item-relative paths stay `bindings` losses.
2. Events: `weft:On.<Event>="{x:Bind ViewModel.a_bCommand}"` → `on-<event>="a.b"` (checked against the kind's events), `On.Submit` on a button → `submit="true"`; native `Command="{x:Bind …Command}"` and `Click="{x:Bind ViewModel.a_b}"` (and the change events) → `on-press`/`on-change`. Code-behind handlers stay `actions` losses.
3. Tokens: `{StaticResource|ThemeResource Weft.<path>}` (`.thickness` stripped) on `Spacing`, `RowSpacing`/`ColumnSpacing`, `Padding`, `MaxWidth` → the token props, when the path is a token of the given tokens; else a `tokens` loss.
4. `crates/weft-winui/src/read/layout.rs`: a `Grid` with tracks on one axis that has an `Auto` track (or only `RowDefinitions`) is a `stack`: direction from the axis, children ordered by `Grid.Column`/`Grid.Row`, a star child track → `grow`, the empty star tracks → `justify` (`end`, `center`, `space-between`), spacing → `gap`. The children's shared cross-axis alignment → `align` (a row's `Center` is the default and not read). Other track patterns and mixed alignments are `layout` losses.
5. Tests in `crates/weft-winui/tests/read.rs`: each binding form and mode, each event form, token references, the layout mappings, and every covered screen read back from its XAML alone with no `bindings`/`actions`/`tokens` losses; regenerate `controls.read.*` and `handwritten.read.*`. SPEC §9 "From WinUI" and its loss list updated.

Result: `crates/weft-winui/src/read/values.rs` reads `{x:Bind}` (paths of `ViewModel` or `WeftRoot` with indexes, `Mode=OneWay|TwoWay`, the `Weft.Not`, `Truthy` and `Hide` function bindings through a prefix that resolves to a `using:` namespace) as bindings with each conversion undone, `weft:On.*`, `Command` and bound native events as actions, and `Weft.*` resources as tokens of the given tokens and the prop's type; `crates/weft-winui/src/read/layout.rs` reads a `Grid` drawn for a stack (tracks on one axis, each child alone in one, an `Auto` track if they are columns) as `direction`, `grow` and `justify`, and the children's shared cross-axis alignment as `align`. The tables in `controls.rs` gained the token attributes and the bound events. SPEC §9 "From WinUI" lists the readings and the remaining losses. Every covered screen (the eight `example-*` screens) and both fixtures (`controls`, `data` from T105.2) read back from their XAML alone with no losses, to the same document except the defaults the generator leaves out. The other corpus screens and catalog examples are not generated yet: their kinds wait for T105.3 (value and choice controls), T105.4 (repetition and containers) and T105.5 (layout vocabulary, tokens and materials), which read back their own markers (`material` on a framing `Border`, `Choice.Value`, `Dialog.IsOpen`). T105.5 must keep a row of all-growing children with no gap apart from a one-row `grid`: both are all-star columns, which this reader takes for a `grid`. Tests: `crates/weft-winui/tests/read.rs` (13: the covered pages read back losslessly; each binding form, function and mode; bindings with no Weft form; each event form; events that name no action; token references; Grid tracks as `justify` and `grow`; shared alignment as `align`), with the `controls.read.*` and `handwritten.read.*` goldens updated. Non-test code is about 470 lines.

(3) `src/controls.rs`, its own module so T105.4/T105.5 do not conflict: the kinds above, `<option>`/`<radio>`/`<segment>`/`<menu-item>` built by their parents, literal number bounds through `weft_core::controls`, literal date bounds through `date_text`. (5) Tests: the examples without `<each>` and the corpus screens `account`, `error-state`, `login`, `menu`, `profile`, `signup`, `wizard-step` join `COVERED` (goldens, comment read-back), a fixture of every new prop, refusals. Verify with `moon run root:changed`, then the full check.

SPEC §9 "To WinUI": the rows of these kinds. 2. `crates/weft-winui/src/generate/containers.rs` (own module beside T105.3's `controls`): `section` a card-look `StackPanel` with the `region` landmark and the `header` slot row first; `dialog` a `ContentDialog` with `weft:Dialog.IsOpen` two-way and the `actions` slot row last, `modal` a marker; `grid` a `Grid` with `columns` star columns, children placed row by row, `gap` as `RowSpacing`/`ColumnSpacing`; `list` a `ListView SelectionMode="None"` of `ListViewItem`s (`ordered` a marker, `item` `on-press` through `On.Press`); `table` a `StackPanel` of a header `Grid` of `column` buttons and one `Grid` per `row` (`sort`, `selected` markers); `tabs` a `TabView` of `TabViewItem`s with `weft:Choice.Value` (after T105.3). 3. `Weft.cs`: `Dialog.IsOpen`, the new `Props` markers, `On` hooks for `ListViewItem`, `TabView` in `Choice`. 4. Reader: the fixed attributes these kinds write, `Dialog.IsOpen`, `Choice.Value` on `TabView`, table rows and cells. 5. Goldens (`PENDING` shrinks), a `containers` fixture for what no example covers, read-back tests; `moon run root:changed`, then the full check.

`<each>` as an `ItemsRepeater` with a typed `DataTemplate` (loop variables in the data typing, `WeftRoot` and `WeftParent` reads, `Each.Id`/`Each.As` markers, the parent's orientation and spacing), the `empty` slots of `list` and `table` with `weft:Weft.Empty`, `<each>` in a `ListView` and in a table, and `read_winui` reading an `ItemsRepeater` with its markers back into `<each>`.
