(2) `data.rs`: loop scopes, so `$todo.title` is `title`, `$.x` inside a loop `WeftRoot.x` and an enclosing loop's item `WeftParent.…`; `in` makes a list of an item class. (3) `generate`: `<each>` as an `ItemsRepeater` with the parent panel's orientation and spacing, a `ListView` whose only child is one `<each>` of one `item` binds `ItemsSource` (the item a `ContentControl` of type `list item`), rows of a table repeat, and an `<each>` anywhere else (grid, row, tabs, menu, select) is refused; the `empty` slot is the list's `Footer` or the table's last panel, its `Visibility` through `weft:Weft.Empty`. (4) `Weft.cs`: `Each` markers, `Weft.Empty`, a press on a templated list item. (5) Reader: an `ItemsRepeater` or `ListView` with `weft:Each.As` is an `<each>`, relative paths read against the loop names. (6) Tests: goldens for the corpus screens and the `list` and `table` examples, a `repetition` fixture (nested loops, `WeftParent`, bound state with the empty slot), refusals, reader tests; `cargo test -p weft-winui --all-features`, then the Windows job. `menu-item` and `option` examples stay pending: a `MenuFlyout` and a `ComboBox` cannot hold a repeater beside static items.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/108. Eight more screens generate (`corpus-data-table`, `corpus-leaderboard`, `corpus-orders`, `corpus-search-results`, `corpus-tabs`, `corpus-todo-list`, `example-list`, `example-table`), with a `repetition` fixture of nested loops, `WeftParent` and bound empty slots, and they read back from the XAML alone without losses.

Found when T105.10 closed: an `<each>` inside `select`, `combobox` or `menu` does not generate, so `corpus-booking`, `example-menu-item` and `example-option` stay in `PENDING` (`crates/weft-winui/tests/common/mod.rs`). (1) SPEC §9 To and From WinUI, `docs/winui-design.md` and `research.md` §23 rows first, with sources. (2) `Weft.cs`: `Each.Source`, `Each.Template` and `Each.At` on a `ComboBox` or `MenuFlyout`: the helper loads the typed `DataTemplate` once per item of the list, sets the item as the element's `DataContext` (compiled template bindings follow the root's `DataContextChanged`), inserts it after the `At` static items, refills when the list changes, and shows the choice's value again. (3) Generator (`generate/repetition.rs`, `generate/controls.rs`): an `<each>` of one `option` or `menu-item` among a choice's options or a menu's items writes those properties, its markers and the template; a second `<each>` or one of other children stays refused. (4) Reader: a `ComboBox` or `MenuFlyout` with `Each.As` and a bound `Each.Source` reads the template's item back as an `<each>` at `At`. (5) Tests: goldens for `corpus-booking`, `example-menu-item`, `example-option` (PENDING shrinks), refusals, a reader test; `moon run root:changed`, the full check, then the Windows `winui` job.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/119. `corpus-booking`, `example-menu-item` and `example-option` generate, match goldens and read back from the XAML alone without losses; the `repetition` fixture adds a select and a menu with static items before the `<each>` (`Each.At="1"`).

### T105.15. WinUI colour tokens, materials and tilt

Split from T105.5. `WeftTokens.xaml` beyond the dimensions (colours with `ThemeDictionaries` for light, dark and high contrast), `material` as an `AcrylicBrush` on a `Border` (with `padding` inside it), and the 3D tilt (`perspective`, `rotate-x`, `rotate-y`, `rotate-z`). 1. Spec first: SPEC §9 "To WinUI" and "From WinUI" (colour and material resources, the material `Border`, the tilt `Border` and `Transform3D`, `weft:Tilt.Centered`), `docs/winui-design.md` § Tokens and the tilt row, `research.md` §23 rows for the facts checked (perspective on a parent, rotation sense, theme dictionary keys).

Generator: `material` wraps the stack or grid in a `Border` with `Background="{ThemeResource Weft.<path>}"` holding its `Padding` and `MaxWidth`; the tilt is a `CompositeTransform3D` on the element (or its material `Border`) with `weft:Tilt.Centered`, under a `Border` with a `PerspectiveTransform3D` when `perspective` is set; layout attributes and `Visibility` go on the outermost wrapper. `Tilt` helper in `Weft.cs`.
5. Reader: those `Border`s and `Transform3D` property elements read back into `material`, `padding`, `max-width`, `hidden`, `perspective` and the rotations.
6. Tests: golden files for `glass`, `tilt`, `showroom` (out of `PENDING`), generator unit tests, round trips both ways, tokens golden; `moon run root:changed`, then the full check; the `winui` CI job builds the goldens.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/120. `corpus-glass`, `corpus-tilt` and `corpus-showroom` generate, match their goldens, build in the Windows `winui` job and read back from the XAML alone without losses. `WeftTokens.xaml` writes colours and materials in `Light`, `Dark` and `HighContrast` theme dictionaries; `weft-catalog` has one sRGB channel reader that `weft-swiftui` now shares. ### T105.18. WinUI per-page tokens and reflowing repeats

Split from T105.15 when it was claimed. Per-page tokens with `export.winui.sharedTokens` and `--shared-tokens`/`--no-shared-tokens` (a key ships with its behaviour: SPEC §10.6 row, `crates/weft-catalog/src/settings.rs`, regenerated `schemas/weft.schema.json`): with `false` the page's `Page.Resources` holds only the tokens it uses, and `import_winui` compares those entries by key and type. And `weft:ColumnsLayout` in `Weft.cs` for an `<each>` in a grid with `min-column-width`, generated and read back. Done when both are generated, read back, covered by tests and built in the Windows job.

**Execution plan.**

1. Spec first: SPEC §9 "To WinUI"/"From WinUI" (per-page `Page.Resources`, `ColumnsLayout`, the key-and-type comparison) and the §10.6 row of `export.winui.sharedTokens`; `docs/winui-design.md`; `research.md` §23 on `NonVirtualizingLayout` and page resources, with Microsoft docs URLs and dates.
2. `crates/weft-winui/src/tokens.rs`: build the resources as `xaml::Element`s (with text content) so `WeftTokens.xaml` and a page's `Page.Resources` share one writer. `GenerateOptions` gains `shared_tokens` and `appearance`; the generator records every token it references and, with `shared_tokens: false`, writes only those into `Page.Resources` (a referenced token WinUI has no form for is refused).
3. `import_winui`: regenerate in the mode the page shows (a `Page.Resources` element) and compare its entries by element type and `x:Key` only; `read_winui` reads the generator's `Weft.*` page resources as nothing to lose.
4. `Weft.cs`: `ColumnsLayout`, a `NonVirtualizingLayout` sharing the column arithmetic with `ColumnsPanel`. A `grid` with `min-column-width` whose only child is an `<each>` of one element becomes an `ItemsRepeater` laid out by it; `read_winui` reads it back as the `grid` and its `<each>`.
5. CLI: `weft winui --shared-tokens/--no-shared-tokens`, precedence flag > `export.winui.sharedTokens` > default (true in a project, false without one); `settings.rs` row, regenerated `schemas/weft.schema.json`, `docs/cli.md`, `docs/projects.md`, the crate README.
6. Tests: a `tokens` fixture generated with per-page tokens and a repeated reflowing grid in the `repetition` fixture (goldens, both read-backs, the Windows build); unit tests for the token subset, the refusal, the loose comparison and an edited value; CLI tests for each option and the precedence. Verify with `moon run root:changed`, then the full check, and the green `winui` job.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/122. `weft winui --no-shared-tokens` (or `export.winui.sharedTokens` `false`, or no project) gives a page whose `Page.Resources` holds only the tokens it references, written as `WeftTokens.xaml` writes them; a referenced token WinUI has no form for is refused. `import_winui` regenerates in the mode the page shows and compares those resources by element and `x:Key`; `read_winui` skips a `Page.Resources` of `Weft.` entries. A `min-column-width` grid whose only child is an `<each>` of one element is an `ItemsRepeater` with a `weft:ColumnsLayout` (a `NonVirtualizingLayout` sharing `ColumnsPanel`'s arithmetic in `Weft.cs`) and reads back as the grid. The `tokens` fixture and the repetition fixture's photo grid have goldens that read back both ways and build in the Windows `winui` job.

The first run caught a real fault: x:Bind cannot name a C# keyword, so the generator now refuses keyword data names (https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/actions/runs/38656182916).

### T105.17. WinUI data paths named like C# keywords

Found when T105.10 closed: `corpus-account` binds `$.account.public`, and the generator refuses it because `public` is a C# keyword. The first decision, the C# verbatim identifier `@public`, cannot work: in the open-source XAML compiler (microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml `main` @ 866ba81a, checked 2026-10-17) the `'@' Identifier_or_keyword` branch of `src/XamlCompiler/BuildTasks/BindingPath/CSharpIdentifier.g4` is commented out, `BindingPath.g4` accepts no `@`, and `PropertyStepCodeGenerator` in `src/XamlCompiler/BuildTasks/CodeGenerators/BindPathStepCodeGenerators.cs` writes property names into the generated `.g.cs` unescaped (CS1055/CS0106 on `class` and `event` in T105.7's run 38656182916). The creator's new decision: a C# reserved keyword gets a `_` suffix, so the view-model property is `public_` and x:Bind reads `ViewModel.account.public_`; a Weft name that is already a keyword followed by underscores (a real `public_`) gets one more (`public__`), and the importer strips exactly one `_` from a name that is a keyword followed by one or more underscores. Data paths and the round trip stay unchanged; only the C# member changes. Contextual keywords stay ordinary names. Done when `corpus-account` leaves `PENDING`, its golden builds in the Windows job, it round-trips, and tests cover the suffix, the double suffix and the importer's stripping.

**Execution plan.** (1) `crates/weft-winui/src/data.rs`: replace the keyword refusal with `member(name)` (Weft name → C# member) and `weft_name(member)` (its inverse), checked against the official reserved-keyword list; `walk` writes the member into the x:Bind path. (2) `src/csharp.rs`: properties, backing fields and the list accesses use the member; `WeftPath` keeps the Weft name. (3) `src/read/values.rs`: `data_path` maps each member back with `weft_name`. (4) Unit tests for both functions and the bijection over every keyword; a generator test for `$.public`, `$.public_` and `$.a.class` and a read-back test; `corpus-account` out of `PENDING` with its goldens. (5) SPEC §9 (to and from WinUI), `AGENT-SPEC.md` if it mentions the refusal, `docs/winui-design.md` (the rule, the keyword list with its source). Verify with `moon run root:changed`, then the full check, then the Windows `winui` job in CI.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/121. `corpus-account` generates (`ViewModel.account.public_`), its goldens join the Windows build, and it round-trips through `import_winui` and `read_winui`. `data::member` adds the `_` to all 77 reserved keywords (alone or already followed by `_`), `data::weft_name` strips exactly one on read-back; unit tests cover every keyword and its suffixed forms, `tests/winui.rs` the generated members and x:Bind paths, `tests/read.rs` the stripping and the round trip of a keyword screen. SPEC §9, `docs/winui-design.md` and `research.md` record the rule and why `@` fails.

### T72. One `weft` command again; batch ids fail at the source

`packages/core/package.json` exposed a second, reduced TS `weft` bin (only `validate`/`fmt`, no project support) with a stale header comment — anyone resolving `weft` through `@weft/core` got usage errors for flags the real Rust CLI accepts. The bin is gone and `cli.ts`'s header now says what it is (a test driver, not the command; `docs/cli.md` names the real one). `bench/src/provider.ts` indexed batch results with `Number(r.custom_id.slice(1))` — a non-conforming id wrote to index `NaN` and surfaced later as "no result for rNaN"; the index is validated with `Number.isInteger` and bounds now and fails naming the id. Found by the 2026-10-07 audit.
Model: ZCode / GLM-5.3 · Status: done 2026-10-08 · Priority: P2 · Complexity: 1 · Files: `packages/core/package.json`, `packages/core/src/cli.ts`, `bench/src/provider.ts`
Check: `moon run core:test` — 199 passed (the TS CLI still drives them); `moon run root:typecheck root:lint` — clean.

### T15.2. Surfaces for the Custom Elements Manifest importer

T15.1 delivers `import_cem` in `weft-import` (library, tests, SPEC section 9). This task makes it reachable: `weft import-cem <manifest.json> [--name] [--version] [--catalog]` prints the catalog (pretty JSON) on stdout and the losses and diagnostics on stderr, reading the file only when it is at most `MAX_MANIFEST_LENGTH` bytes; the `import.cem.outDir` setting (SPEC section 10.6); the WebAssembly and native bindings (`weft-binding`, `weft-wasm`, `weft-node`, `@weft/core` types), which the creator wanted from TypeScript too; `docs/importing.md` and `docs/cli.md`. ### T13.1. json-render export

The json-render half of T13, export side: export a Weft document to a json-render spec (`{ root, elements, state }`, the Weft catalog as the json-render catalog), with its losses in SPEC §9, in `crates/weft-interop`. The import was split off as T13.2 when the code of both directions came to about 700 lines against the 500-line limit. Done when every corpus screen exports to a spec that passes json-render's structural checks and the export losses are listed.

Execution plan:

1. Primary source: `vercel-labs/json-render` at commit `fc2a696` (2026-10-01) and `@json-render/core` 0.21.0 on npm. A spec is `{ root, elements, state? }`; an element is `{ type, props, children?, slots?, visible?, on?, repeat?, watch? }`; props read data with `$state`, `$item`, `$bindState`, `$bindItem` and `$cond` (JSON Pointers); `repeat` repeats an element's children over a state array. This matches the card.
2. `SPEC.md` §9 first, in the same commit as the code: "To json-render" and its loss table. The element `type` is the Weft kind and the props are Weft's own (tokens as `{ "token": … }`), so the mapping is one to one: bindings become `$state`/`$item` (`$bindState`/`$bindItem` on writable props, `$cond` for a negated one), `hidden` is `visible`, `on-*` is `on` with the element id and loop item as params, `<each>` is an element of type `each` with `repeat`, named slots are `slots`, text content is the `text` prop.
3. Code in `crates/weft-interop/src/json_render/export.rs`: `to_json_render(document, catalog, state)`. It reuses the pointer conversion in `paths.rs`; the A2UI exporter's id and number helpers move to the crate root so both exporters share them.
4. Tests in `crates/weft-snapshots/tests/interop.rs`: every corpus screen and catalog example exports to a spec that passes the checks of json-render's `validateSpec` (with the corpus sample data as `state`); snapshots pin the corpus specs and the losses; a screen built to lose reports exactly its losses.
5. The CLI commands and `weft.json` keys are not part of this card.
6. Verify with `moon run root:changed`, then the full check.

Result: `crates/weft-interop/src/json_render/export.rs` (`to_json_render(document, catalog, state)` returns the spec and its losses) and SPEC §9 "To json-render" with its loss table (`bindings` for a binding to an outer repetition, `text` for a text run beside elements). The id helpers moved to `crates/weft-interop/src/ids.rs` and the number writer to the crate root, shared with the A2UI exporter. Sources: `vercel-labs/json-render` at `fc2a696` and `@json-render/core` 0.21.0; the format matched the card. No CLI command or `weft.json` key yet. The import is T13.2.

### T12.1. Constrained generation: document JSON Schema from a catalog

First part of T12 (constrained generation), split off with T12.2 (surfaces) and T12.3 (benchmark) when it was claimed. Provider structured-output modes take a JSON Schema and constrain decoding to it; a schema built from the catalog keeps a model writing canonical JSON (SPEC §3) from producing an unknown kind, an undeclared prop, a wrong enum value, an undeclared slot or a child kind that is not allowed. Scope: a SPEC subsection after §3, "JSON Schema of the canonical form" (construct-to-schema mapping, what stays in the semantic layer, dialect 2020-12, deterministic output), a row in the `research.md` decisions table, and the generator `document_schema(catalog, options) -> Json` in `crates/weft-catalog/src/document_schema.rs`. Creator's decisions: the schema describes the catalog only (no project token, action or data-path enums for now); `x-` extensions are left out of the constrained schema; the Zod `documentJsonSchema()` stays untouched. Done when the canonical JSON of every corpus screen and catalog example validates against the schema of the core catalog and of `examples/project`'s merged catalog, a negative set (unknown kind, undeclared prop, wrong enum value, child kind not allowed, undeclared slot, missing required prop, number out of range) is rejected by both the schema and the validator with matching codes, and generating twice is byte-identical, pinned by a snapshot.

Result: SPEC §3.1 maps each catalog construct to JSON Schema 2020-12: a closed object per kind in `$defs` (`kind` as `const`, `id` by pattern, `props`, `on`, `slots`, `children` in canonical order), props as `anyOf` of the literal form by type (`integer` with `minimum`/`maximum`, `enum` of `values` or `states`), `{bind}` unless `bindable: false`, `{bind, not: true}` only on a read-only boolean, `{token}` only (no literal) on a token prop; events and slots as closed objects; the admitted elements of every list from `allowedChildren` and `allowedParents` (kinds without `allowedParents` collected once in `$defs/Node`, the root kind admitted only at the root, its `weft` prop left out); one transparent `each:P` / `each:P:S` entry per list that admits `each`; the universal attributes shared as `universal:<name>` entries. It lists what stays in the validator (`W213`, `W221`, `W301`, `W305`, `W309`–`W311`, `W313`, `W317`, and the project's tokens, actions and data schema) and the two places the schema is stricter than strict validation (extensions, a bound `state` on a kind without states). `research.md` §20 records the decisions with the JSON Schema 2020-12 sources. `weft-core` exports `universal_props()`, the validator's own table, so the generator cannot drift from it. Tests (`crates/weft-snapshots/tests/document_schema.rs`, 8 tests, reusing the `jsonschema` 0.58.5 dev dependency and the corpus helpers): both schemas pass the 2020-12 meta-schema; the 59 corpus screens and catalog examples pass the core schema, and the same plus the two project screens pass the project schema; 31 negative cases fail the schema and give the expected code in strict validation (the seven of the done criteria plus `W201`, `W202`, `W204`, `W206`, `W209`, `W212`, `W214`–`W218`, `W222`, `W303`, `W304`, `W312`, `W314`, `W403`); the extension's `rating` range and wider `button.variant`; the id, binding, token, action and loop-variable patterns agree with the grammar functions; the output is byte-identical across runs and catalog key orders, pinned by insta snapshots of both schemas. The core schema is 58 KB as compact JSON (44 KB without descriptions); whether providers take it whole is T12.3's question. `AGENT-SPEC.md` is unchanged: no agent-facing surface exists until T12.2. The plugin `wasm-web` bundles were rebuilt; the full check exits 0.

### T12.3. Constrained generation: benchmark

Third part of T12 (constrained generation): measure whether constrained JSON beats free markup on validity and edit success, and what it costs in output tokens. Out of scope (all of T12): any change to the markup format or the validator, streaming of constrained JSON, grammar-level decoding for markup, and making constrained JSON the format agents are told to use. Scope: `Provider.complete(prompt, { schema? })` for Anthropic and OpenAI-compatible servers; two formats, `weft-json` (free canonical JSON) and `weft-json-constrained`, to separate "JSON instead of markup" from "constrained instead of free"; a JSON primer and an adapter that reuses the Weft neutral tree; corpus JSON derived from `screen.weft` at run time; the same repair rule; `test.md` method sections, a trial run with its History row, and a results section in the T8 evaluation report. Creator's answers (2026-10-12): three-way comparison; Anthropic plus a local LM Studio model; the results are a section of `bench/EVALUATION.md`; a provider that cannot take the schema whole is reported as unsupported with the reasons, without a reduced profile; no project-data narrowing now. Done when a mocked-provider test shows that the schema is sent and that the reply is scored by the same checks as every other format, and a trial run is in `test.md` History with its raw results in `bench/results/`, and the comparison (valid and success, first try and after repair, mean output tokens) for markup, free JSON and constrained JSON is in the evaluation report.

Result: `research.md` §20 records, with sources, what Anthropic structured outputs and LM Studio (llama.cpp grammars, Outlines) accept, and how the core document schema measures against the Anthropic limits: it breaks all four (36 self-reaching `$defs`, 6 numeric bounds, 132 `anyOf`, 416 optional properties), so `bench/src/schema-limits.ts` lets `run.ts` report Anthropic as unsupported for the constrained format before any call. `bench/src/provider.ts` sends the schema as `output_config.format` (Anthropic) or `response_format` (OpenAI-compatible, strict); the batching provider refuses a schema rather than drop it; a constrained LM Studio reply filed as reasoning is read as the answer. The two formats are opt-in through `--formats`, edit-only, share one primer, and score through `parseWeftJson` (strict validation, the markup's neutral tree). The trial (LM Studio, Bonsai 27B, login screen, 3 samples, commit `7caefe55`, `bench/results/edit-2026-10-12T18-31-58-922Z.*`): valid / success after repair / mean output tokens — markup 88.9% / 55.6% / 240, free JSON 100% / 100% / 648, constrained JSON 66.7% / 33.3% / 380. The schema was accepted and obeyed (every JSON reply passes it under Ajv 2020-12); the invalid constrained replies break cross-node rules no schema states (W301, W313), and the grammar admitted schema-legal nonsense props. Confounds (no thinking under the schema, pretty-printed free JSON) and the reading are in `bench/EVALUATION.md`. An Anthropic run of markup against free JSON waits for the creator's approval of its cost (about $0.17 for the same trial on Sonnet 5.5).

### T14.1. Figma pull over the REST API

Read a frame of a Figma file through the REST API and turn it back into Weft with the existing read-back, so a screen built by the plugin comes back byte-identical without opening Figma. Done when the pull reads a frame served by a fake HTTP server (built from the corpus through the fake Plugin API) back byte-identical, the token never appears in output or errors, and the command and its `weft.json` keys are documented.

Execution plan:

1. Check the endpoints, auth header, plugin data parameter, limits and errors against Figma's official REST docs; record each fact with its URL and date in `packages/figma/README.md`.
2. `packages/figma/src/layer.ts`: `figmaLayers` and `styleKey` read a narrow read-only node shape (`ReadNode`) that Plugin API nodes satisfy, so REST nodes reuse the same wrapper instead of a second copy.
3. `packages/figma/src/rest.ts` (subpath `@weft/figma/rest`, kept out of the plugin bundle): parse a `GET /v1/files/:key/nodes` response (`plugin_data=<plugin id>`) into `ReadNode`s, checking every field as untrusted input; fetch the frame, then the main components of its instances in one more request; bound the response size; map 400/403/404/429 to clear errors with the token redacted; parse a `figma.com` URL into a file key and node id.
4. `plugins/shared/scripts/figma-pull.ts` (bundled as `dist/figma-pull.js`): `figma-pull <figma URL | file key> [out.weft] [--node <id>] [--plugin-id <id>] [--force] [--project weft.json | --no-project]`. The token comes only from `FIGMA_TOKEN`; requests go only to `https://api.figma.com`. Prints the loss table like `import`.
5. Settings: `import.figma.outDir` and `import.figma.pluginId` (SPEC §10.6 rows, `crates/weft-catalog/src/settings.rs`, regenerated schema, the TypeScript `Settings` type).
6. A `/weft:figma-pull` skill for Claude Code and Cursor.
7. Tests: corpus screens through a local HTTP server byte-identical; a designer edit; URL parsing; errors and redaction; the script's exit codes. Docs: `docs/cli.md`, `docs/claude-code-plugin.md`, `packages/figma/README.md`.

Result: `@weft/figma/pull` (`packages/figma/src/pull.ts`, `rest.ts`) reads a frame with `GET /v1/files/:key/nodes?ids=…&plugin_data=<plugin id>` and the `X-Figma-Token` header, then the main components of its instances (`depth=1`, 200 ids per request), checks the JSON as untrusted input, and runs it through the plugin's own read-back: `figmaLayers` and `styleKey` now take a read-only node shape (`ReadNode`) that both Plugin API nodes and REST nodes satisfy. Responses are bounded (50 MB, `MAX_NODES`), 403/404/429 and other failures give clear messages (`Retry-After` on 429, the server's `err` without control characters and bounded), and the token is redacted from every error. A `figma.com` link (design, file or branch link, `node-id` with `-` or `:`) or a bare file key with `--node` names the frame; other hosts are refused. The plugins' `figma-pull` script (`plugins/shared/scripts/figma-pull.ts`, bundled as `dist/figma-pull.js`) and the `/weft:figma-pull` and `/weft-figma-pull` skills write `<screen id>.weft` and print the loss table; the token comes from `FIGMA_TOKEN` only. Settings `import.figma.outDir` and `import.figma.pluginId` (default `"weft-development"`, the manifest id) are in SPEC §10.6, `settings.rs` and the schema. REST facts with sources are in `packages/figma/README.md`. Not a Rust `weft figma pull` subcommand: the Figma conversion is TypeScript, as for the other plugin scripts.

### T14.2. Weft source in shared plugin data

Move the Weft source the Figma plugin stores on layers, components, pages, variables and collections from private plugin data (`setPluginData`, readable only by the plugin that wrote it) to shared plugin data (`setSharedPluginData` under the namespace `weft`, readable by any plugin, the Figma MCP server's `use_figma` and the REST API with `plugin_data=shared`). Files built before the change keep working. Done when a build writes only shared data, a file with only private data reads back the same and is migrated, and the REST pull reads shared data first.

Execution plan:

1. Check the shared plugin data API (namespace rules, size, empty string removes) and the REST `sharedPluginData` field against Figma's docs; record them with URL and date in `packages/figma/README.md`.
2. `packages/figma/src/data.ts`: `NAMESPACE = "weft"` (Penpot's namespace too) and `dataOf(node)`, a `PluginData` view that reads shared data first and falls back to private data; a value found only in private data is moved to shared data when it is read (on load) and private data is cleared when a key is written. Migration is best effort: a node that cannot be written (a REST node, a read-only file) still reads.
3. Route every plugin data access in `packages/figma/src` (`build.ts`, `layer.ts`, `library.ts`, `modes.ts`) through `dataOf`; add the shared methods to `FPluginData` (checked against `@figma/plugin-typings` by `api-types.test.ts`).
4. REST: ask `plugin_data=shared,<plugin id>`; `rest.ts` reads `sharedPluginData.weft` first, then `pluginData[<plugin id>]` for frames built before the change. `import.figma.pluginId` stays for those frames.

### T17.0. Extension catalogs: design

First part of T17 (extension catalogs), split off with T17.1 (spec and loader), T17.2 (tools and docs) and T17.3 (distribution and registry) when it was claimed. Docs only: no code, no `SPEC.md`, `AGENT-SPEC.md` or catalog change. A project could extend the core catalog with exactly one file, so a component library (for example one imported from a Custom Elements Manifest) and the project's own kinds could not be loaded together, and nothing said how catalogs are namespaced, published, found or listed. Done when the creator has approved `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md`, with T17's open questions answered.

Result: `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md`, approved by the creator with the recommended answer to all twelve open questions, now its **Decisions** section. Kinds are namespaced by hyphen prefixes that the catalog author declares (`"prefix": "acme"` owns `acme-*`), so no document, parser, format version or generator changes; a catalog with a prefix is a library that defines only its own kinds, and the one catalog without a prefix, the project catalog, alone extends core and library kinds, widen-only. Catalogs gain optional `prefix` and `requires` (Cargo's compatibility rule); `catalog` in `weft.json` becomes an ordered array of file and `{ "package" }` entries; new codes `W711`–`W714`; committed files first, npm packages with a `weft.catalog` field found by walking up `node_modules`, never over the network; a curated `docs/catalogs.md` and the npm keyword `weft-catalog` instead of a registry service. A worked example covers `examples/project` with the `acme-ui` CEM import and the hand-written `shop` catalog. `research.md` §22 holds the prior art (custom element names and scoped registries, Custom Elements Manifest, XML namespaces, ESLint and shadcn namespaces, Slint imports, Swift modules, A2UI catalogs, npm and Node package files and lookup, Subresource Integrity, Cargo version requirements, JSON Schema `$id`, the shadcn directory and SchemaStore, Figma and Penpot libraries) with sources and dates; one fact (Swift module-qualified type names) is marked **unverified**. The §9 registry row records the decision.

### T17.1. Extension catalogs: spec and loader

T17's format and loader, by decisions 1–6 and 11 of `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md`. File entries only; package entries come with T17.3.

- `SPEC.md`: §5 gains the optional `prefix` (one name segment; not `x`, `weft`, a core kind or a core kind's first segment) and `requires` (catalog name → version, Cargo's compatibility rule); §8, the advertisement lists every catalog; §10.2, `catalog` is a file name or an ordered array of at most 32 file names (`W701` beyond); §10.4, the library and project-catalog roles and the three merge steps (core, libraries in order, the project catalog last, widen-only against the definition it extends); §6.2, `W711` (two catalogs claim one name, prefix or kind, naming both), `W712` (bad or reserved prefix, or a second catalog without one), `W713` (a kind outside its owner), `W714` (unmet `requires`, a warning).
- `crates/weft-catalog/src/project.rs`: several catalogs, `CATALOG_MEMBERS` with `prefix` and `requires`; `Project` keeps the loaded catalogs (`name`, `version`, `prefix`, `source`) and, per kind, the defining catalog and the catalogs that extended it; the merged `Catalog` keeps the project catalog's name and version (else the last library's). Never panics. The diagnostics registry in both engines.
- Differential fixtures for the catalog loader (regenerate with `WEFT_UPDATE_FIXTURES=1`).
- `examples/project`: `catalogs/acme-ui.catalog.json`, the `import-cem` output of the `acme-ui.json` fixture with `prefix: "acme"` and `requires` added by hand (T17.2 regenerates it with `--prefix` and must produce the same file); `catalog.json` gains `requires` and widens `acme-button.variant`; `weft.json` lists both; a screen uses kinds of all three catalogs; broken project variants give each new code. The T12 document schema tests cover the merged catalog.
- Done when T17's first three done criteria hold for the loader (both extensions validate in strict mode; a kind claimed by two catalogs names both; the TypeScript and Rust fixtures agree) and `cargo nextest`, clippy and the TypeScript tests pass.

Result: `SPEC.md` §5 gives catalogs an optional `prefix` (a library owns the `prefix-*` kinds) and `requires` (Cargo's compatibility rule, checked with the `semver` crate's caret comparator); §10.2 lets `catalog` be one file or an ordered array of at most 32 (`W701` beyond, pointers `#/catalog/<i>`); §10.4 merges the core, then libraries in array order, then the one unprefixed project catalog, which alone extends core and library kinds, widen-only against the definition it extends; §6.2 adds `W711` (a name, prefix or kind claimed twice, naming both), `W712` (bad or reserved prefix, or a second unprefixed catalog), `W713` (a kind outside its owner) and `W714` (unmet `requires`, a warning); §8 lists every catalog. `crates/weft-catalog/src/project.rs` loads that, and `Project` carries `catalogs` (name, version, prefix, source) and `kinds` (the defining catalog and the catalogs that extended it), through `weft-binding` to `@weft/catalog`. Both diagnostics registries and `AGENT-SPEC.md` cover the new codes; the `catalog` setting schema is a `oneOf`. `examples/project` gains `catalogs/acme-ui.catalog.json` (the `import-cem` output with `prefix` and `requires` added), a `catalog.json` 1.1.0 that requires both and widens `acme-button.variant`, both in `weft.json`, and `screens/order.weft` with kinds of all three catalogs; the T12 document schema tests pin the merged catalog. Choices made where the design was silent (PR body): a non-string `prefix` or a `requires` that is not an object of versions is `W706` and drops the catalog; a catalog named `weft-core` is `W711`; `source` is absent for inline content; `kinds` also lists core kinds; ownership is checked before name claims for library kinds; `requires` is checked after all claims and before the merge.

### T17. Extension catalogs

Parent of T17.1–T17.3, which carry the work (the task was split this way when T17.0 was claimed). T17.0–T17.3 are done (`done.md`); for T17.0 the creator approved `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md` with its twelve decisions, which T17.1–T17.3 build. This card holds the context, scope and done criteria they share.

A project can extend the core catalog with exactly one file today. Real hosts combine several sources: the core, one or more component libraries, and their own kinds. So a project needs to load several catalogs at once without their kinds colliding. Once catalogs travel between projects, two more questions need answers: how a catalog is published and found, and who keeps the registry. `research.md` §9 lists "Who keeps a registry of extension catalogs" as open; `docs/catalog-and-tokens.md` and `docs/what-is-weft.md` both say there is no registry; `docs/fragments-design.md` defers namespaces and sharing across projects to this task.

**Context.**
- SPEC §10.2: `catalog` is one file name. §10.4 merges that one extension into the core catalog: new kinds need a whole definition, core kinds are extended by merge or join, the extension may only widen the core (§8 rules via `crates/weft-catalog/src/diff.rs`), codes `W706` (invalid) and `W707` (narrows), and the merged catalog takes the extension's `name` and `version`.
- Code: `Loader::extend_catalog` and `extend_definition` in `crates/weft-catalog/src/project.rs`. `Project.catalog` is a single `Catalog` with no record of which catalog each kind came from. The CLI's `--catalog` loads one whole catalog that replaces the project's (`load_catalog` in `crates/weft-cli/src/main.rs`). `weft_capabilities` already returns `catalogs: [{ name, version }]`, the core first and then the extension (T10); `weft_catalog` lists kinds with no source.
- Kind names follow `[a-z][a-z0-9]*(-[a-z0-9]+)*` (SPEC §2), so there is no `:`. `x-<vendor>-` names are opaque extensions that no catalog may define (§8, §10.4).
- T15.1/T15.2 import a Custom Elements Manifest into an extension whose kinds are the custom-element tags (`acme-button`), with `--name`/`--version`/`--catalog` and the `import.cem.*` settings (`crates/weft-import/src/cem.rs`, `crates/weft-cli/src/cem.rs`, fixture `crates/weft-import/tests/fixtures/cem/acme-ui.json`). A library catalog and a project catalog cannot be used together today.
- Generators already name extension kinds by convention (SwiftUI: `promo-card` becomes `PromoCardView`, SPEC §9); the Figma and Penpot libraries build one component set per kind.
- `@weft/catalog` is `private` in `packages/catalog/package.json`; nothing is published anywhere yet.
- Documents and validators never touch the network (AGENTS.md). Project file names stay inside the project directory (§10.2, `W703`); decision 8 makes package lookup in parent `node_modules` folders a narrow, documented exception.

**Scope.**
- Design (done, T17.0): `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md`, approved, with its **Decisions** section as the contract for the rest.
- Build: SPEC §5, §10.2 (`catalog` also takes an ordered array), §10.4, and new `W7xx` codes for cross-catalog conflicts; the loader keeps which catalog each kind came from, with differential fixtures; CLI `--catalog` can be repeated; MCP `project.catalog` accepts an array, `weft_capabilities` lists every catalog, `weft_catalog` shows each kind's catalog; `import-cem` gets a namespace option with its `import.cem.*` key (SPEC §10.6, `settings.rs`, `schemas/weft.schema.json`); `AGENT-SPEC.md` §1 and §2.8 and the primer; `docs/projects.md`, `docs/catalog-and-tokens.md`, and a publishing guide; the `research.md` §9 row answered.

**Out of scope.** Hosting a registry service. Network fetching during validation or rendering. Changing the `x-` extension rules. Fragments themselves (T31 part B). New generator features beyond naming namespaced kinds.

**Done when.**
- `examples/project` loads the core plus two extensions (one imported from `acme-ui.json`, one written by hand), and kinds from both validate in strict mode.
- A kind defined by two catalogs gets a diagnostic that names both catalogs, never a panic.
- `weft_capabilities` lists all three catalogs; the TypeScript and Rust differential fixtures agree.
- A catalog shipped in a package directory resolves by the documented convention with no network access.
- `schemas/weft.schema.json` is regenerated; SPEC, AGENT-SPEC and the docs are updated.

**Dependencies.** Done: T31 (project file and settings), T15.1/T15.2 (CEM import), T10 (capabilities). T12: its schema must cover the merged catalogs. Affected: T14/T40 (one design-tool library per catalog), T44 (SwiftUI custom views), T69 milestone 4 (catalog kinds as components), the fragments design (T31 part B).

**Decisions** (creator, recorded in `docs/extension-catalogs-design.md`): hyphen prefixes declared by the catalog author (`"prefix": "acme"`), no aliases; libraries define only their own kinds, and the one catalog without a prefix, the project catalog, alone extends core and library kinds, widen-only; `requires` with Cargo's compatibility rule, unmet as the warning `W714`; `catalog` as a file name or an ordered array of at most 32 file and `{ "package" }` entries, libraries merged before the project catalog; new codes `W711`–`W714`; committed files first, npm packages with a `weft.catalog` field later, found by walking up `node_modules`, never over the network; a curated `docs/catalogs.md` and the npm keyword `weft-catalog` instead of a registry service; one design-tool build grouped by catalog; screens stay silent about their catalogs; a repeated `--catalog` replaces the list, and `import-cem` gets `--prefix` with a hint when it is absent.

**Split.** T17.0: design (done). T17.1: spec and loader for several catalogs and prefixes, with fixtures (done). T17.2: CLI, MCP, `import-cem --prefix`, settings, design tools, AGENT-SPEC and docs (done). T17.3: package entries and lookup, the publishing guide and the curated list (done). T17.1 lands first; T17.2 and T17.3 build on it.

Result: all four children are done. `examples/project` loads the core, `acme-ui` (imported from `acme-ui.json` with `--prefix acme`) and the hand-written `shop` catalog, and kinds of all three validate in strict mode (T17.1); a kind claimed by two catalogs is `W711`, naming both (T17.1); `weft_capabilities` lists all three catalogs with `prefix` and `source`, and the TypeScript and Rust differential fixtures agree (T17.2); a catalog in an npm package resolves through `node_modules` of the project directory or a parent with no network access (T17.3); `schemas/weft.schema.json`, SPEC, AGENT-SPEC and the docs are updated (T17.1–T17.3).

### T73. A CI workflow runs the documented merge gate

The repo had no `.github/` at all: the documented gate (`moon run :test root:typecheck root:lint root:rust-test root:rust-lint root:runtimes`, README.md) ran only by hand, and publishing from GitHub (T32) would have shipped without it. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` now runs the gate on every pull request and push to main: mise installs the pinned toolchain (moon, Rust with wasm32, deno, bun), pnpm installs the dependencies with the frozen lockfile, the Playwright Chromium is installed for the browser leg of `root:runtimes`, and both actions are pinned to full commit SHAs. The first runs of the workflow are its own shakedown: a tooling failure on the runner is fixed on this branch, a gate failure is the gate working.

### T69.1c. Editor C ABI

Sub-task of T69.1 (`crates/weft-studio-ffi`, about 350 lines); the T69.1 card holds the `ws_*` API, the events and the decisions. Measure the open-to-first-frame time of the largest corpus screen in release and record it here; above 500 ms, cache the compiled definition per source.

Execution plan:

1. Workspace: `slint-bindings-ffi` as a git dependency on https://github.com/pyrlyn/slint-bindings at `rev = "8971685e01fee6776e5121dffb13e3eb43709437"` (G1–G2: `SbHost::new`, `SbHost::into_raw`); confirm that `slint-embed` and `slint-dart-interpreter`, path crates under `flutter/` there, resolve from the git checkout, and that the lock file keeps one `i-slint-core`.
2. `crates/weft-studio-ffi`: `crate-type = ["rlib"]`, its own `[lints]` with `unsafe_code = "deny"` and the `extern "C"` module allowed, as `weft-node` does. `src/lib.rs` holds the `ws_*` calls over `weft_studio::Editor` (open, save, state JSON with `"v":0`, events), a guard of its own (panic catch, thread-local last error; `slint-bindings-ffi` keeps its guard private), and `ws_surface_new`: `render_input`, `weft_slint::runtime::compile` with the `fluent` style, `SbHost::new`, `apply_data`, and `perform` connected to an `action` event.
3. `cbindgen.toml`, committed `include/weft_studio.h` and `bindings/csharp/NativeMethods.g.cs`; `tests/bindings.rs` checks both with `abi-drift` (dev-dependency, with `cbindgen` and `csbindgen`), `WEFT_STUDIO_BLESS=1` rewrites them.
4. `tests/e2e.rs`: the done criteria above, through the C ABI only; the static library check builds `--crate-type staticlib` and reads `nm` on macOS and skips elsewhere. A release-only measurement test prints the open-to-first-frame time over the corpus.
5. `toolchain.md` rows (slint-bindings, `abi-drift`, `cbindgen`, `csbindgen`), the moon file groups if `tooling/test/inputs.test.ts` asks, `crates/weft-cli/tests/deps.rs` if it checks every crate. The workspace's `rust.md` lies outside this repository and changes only with the creator's permission: it lists `cbindgen` (as a global tool) and `abi-drift`, not yet `csbindgen` or slint-bindings.
6. `moon run root:changed`, then the full check under the machine-wide lock.

Result: `crates/weft-studio-ffi` holds the `ws_*` calls of the T69.1 card over `weft_studio::Editor` (`src/abi.rs`, the only module that allows unsafe code; a guard of its own catches panics and keeps a thread-local last error, and every call checks that it comes from the editor's thread) and links `slint-bindings-ffi` from https://github.com/pyrlyn/slint-bindings at `8971685`, whose `slint-embed` and `slint-dart-interpreter` path crates resolve from the git checkout. `ws_surface_new` compiles the generated source with the `fluent` style, fills `render.data`, connects `perform` to an `action` event and hands out `SbHost::into_raw`; the handler holds a copy of the event callback, not the editor, so a surface may outlive its editor. Messages for a syntax error or a refused screen list the diagnostics, one per line. `include/weft_studio.h` (cbindgen; it declares `typedef struct SbHost SbHost;` so it compiles beside slint-bindings' header in either order) and `bindings/csharp/NativeMethods.g.cs` (csbindgen, namespace `WeftStudio.Native`, `SbHost*` as `void*` for `SlintHost.Adopt`) are checked by `tests/bindings.rs` with `abi-drift`. Open-to-first-frame in release on Apple Silicon at 480×800: the largest screen, `account`, about 110 ms as the first surface of a process and 40–80 ms after it (compile about 50 ms), so no definition cache; the first launch of a freshly linked binary adds a one-off 3–5 s in the first `SbHost::new`. Slint takes the first thread that draws as the UI thread of the process, so `tests/e2e.rs` draws in one test and `tests/latency.rs` (every corpus screen) is its own binary.

### T69.1e. Windows shell

Sub-task of T69.1 (`studio/windows`, about 350 lines of C#); the T69.1 card holds the `ws_*` API, the events and the decisions. `MainWindow` with a `MenuBar` (Open Ctrl+O, Save Ctrl+S, Save As Ctrl+Shift+S as `KeyboardAccelerator`s), `FileOpenPicker` and `FileSavePicker` from `Microsoft.Windows.Storage.Pickers` (constructed with `AppWindow.Id`, the result's `Path` is the file) behind an `IFilePicker` interface, a `ContentDialog` for errors, slint-bindings' `SlintPanel` with the host from `ws_surface_new`, and P/Invoke from the generated `NativeMethods.g.cs`. Done when MSTest unit tests of the document model pass with a fake picker; a FlaUI (UIA3) smoke test launches the app with a corpus screen, checks the window title and a panel capture that is not one colour, and Save As writes the same bytes; and `studio/test/windows.test.ts` runs `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` on Windows, verified on GitHub's `windows-latest` (decision 4).

Execution plan:

1. `studio/scripts/slint-bindings.ts`: finds the slint-bindings checkout Cargo made of the pinned `rev` (`cargo metadata`, the `manifest_path` of `slint-bindings-ffi`) and writes the gitignored `studio/windows/SlintBindings.g.props` with `SlintBindingsDir`.
2. `studio/windows/Directory.Build.props` and `.targets`: the target triple from the platform, a `BeforeBuild` target that runs `cargo rustc -p weft-studio-ffi --crate-type cdylib --target <triple>` (`--release` for Release) and copies `weft_studio_ffi.dll` next to the output.
3. `WeftStudio.WinUI` (.NET 10, Windows App SDK 2.5.1, unpackaged, self-contained runtime, x64 and ARM64): `Model/` (`Editor` over `NativeMethods.g.cs`, `Document` with `IFilePicker`), `WinUiFilePicker`, `App` (a screen path argument, `--pick <path>` for the smoke test, and a `DllImport` resolver that sends slint-bindings' `slint_bindings_ffi` imports to `weft_studio_ffi`, so the surface and the editor share one library), `MainWindow` (menu, accelerators, `ContentDialog`, `SlintPanel.HostFactory` with `SlintHost.Adopt(ws_surface_new(...))`). The control comes by `ProjectReference` from the checkout, its `SlintNativeDll` pointed at the weft DLL.
4. `WeftStudio.Tests` (MSTest.Sdk, FlaUI.UIA3 5.0.0): model tests with a fake picker (open, cancel, Save As byte-identical, Save without a path asks, a refused open keeps the document and reports the error) and the FlaUI smoke test (`login` from the corpus, title `screen.weft`, a capture below the menu bar with more than one colour, Save As through `--pick` writes the same bytes).
5. `studio/package.json`, `studio/moon.yml` (`studio:test`), `studio/test/windows.test.ts` (skips off Windows or without the .NET SDK), `studio` in `.moon/workspace.yml` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `studio/README.md` (build, manual checklist), `toolchain.md` rows.
6. `ci.yml`: a `studio-windows` job on `windows-latest` beside `winui`, not required: `cargo nextest run -p weft-studio -p weft-studio-ffi` and the Windows leg of `studio:test`, with the window capture as an artifact. That covers T69.1f.
7. `moon run root:changed`, the Windows job green on the pull request, then the full check under the machine-wide lock.

Result: `studio/windows` holds `WeftStudio.WinUI` (WinUI 3, .NET 10, Windows App SDK 2.5.1, unpackaged, x64 and ARM64) and `WeftStudio.Tests` (MSTest, FlaUI.UIA3). The build runs `cargo rustc -p weft-studio-ffi --crate-type cdylib` and copies `weft_studio_ffi.dll` next to the app; slint-bindings' `SlintPanel` comes by `ProjectReference` from the checkout of the pinned `rev` (`studio/scripts/slint-bindings.ts` writes the gitignored `SlintBindings.g.props`), and its `slint_bindings_ffi` imports resolve to `weft_studio_ffi.dll`, so the editor and the surface share one Slint. The `studio-windows` job of `ci.yml` (not required, like `winui`) runs the editor crates' tests and the Windows leg of `studio:test` on `windows-latest`: the model tests and the FlaUI smoke run pass there, and the window capture is kept as an artifact. That job is T69.1f, so it left `roadmap.md`. The first CI runs found two faults no local build could: MSBuild refuses an XML comment that holds a double hyphen (during restore it skipped the broken `Directory.Build.props` and failed later with a misleading error), now checked on every OS by `studio/test/msbuild-xml.test.ts`; and awaiting a WinRT `IAsyncOperation` needs `using System;`.

### T902. Flaky studio-windows smoke test

Bug. `The_app_shows_a_corpus_screen_and_saves_it_as_the_same_bytes` (`studio/windows/WeftStudio.Tests/SmokeTests.cs`) fails now and then on the `studio-windows` job: on main (run 38812071828) and on two PR runs the title stays `screen.weft` for the whole 60 s after Ctrl+Shift+S, so Save As never ran. The window capture taken before the keystroke matches a passing run. Done when the cause is found and fixed (not a longer timeout or a retry), the job passes several reruns in a row, and a failure says what was missing.

Execution plan:

1. Probe PR: repeat the Save As step and record the foreground window and the focus window around the click and the keystroke (`Native.cs`, `GetForegroundWindow`, `GetGUIThreadInfo`), to see which one is not ready when the keystroke is lost.
2. Fix the test to wait, bounded, for that condition before it types, with an assertion that names the missing condition and the windows it found.
3. Rerun the `studio-windows` job several times on the fix PR; record the runs in the PR.

Result: the flake was a real bug. `SlintPanel` (slint-bindings rev 8971685) marks every Control chord handled on `PreviewKeyDown`, so XAML skipped `ProcessKeyboardAccelerators` and the File menu's shortcuts were dead while the panel had focus. The smoke test passed only when its keys overtook the click that focuses the panel; pointer input reaches the panel later than keys sent right behind it, and on a cold launch the click won about half the time (5 of 10 one-launch jobs on the probe PR). An in-app input log on the probe showed both orders. `MainWindow` now matches the menu's own `KeyboardAccelerators` on the root's tunnelling `PreviewKeyDown`, before the panel, and invokes the item. The smoke test clicks into the Email field and waits, bounded, until the panel shows that focus before it types, so it always takes the panel-focused path; without the fix it failed 5 of 5 jobs, with it the `studio-windows` job passed four runs in a row on [#115](https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/115). Follow-up for the creator: the proper fix is in slint-bindings (mark a chord handled only when Slint took it), which needs a pinned rev bump.

Model: Claude Code / claude-opus-5-5 · Status: done · Priority: P1 · Complexity: 2
Check: the `studio-windows` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

### T69.2a. Embedded Slint components

First slice of T69 milestone 2 (the canvas). T69.1 renders one screen as an exported `Window`. Several screens on one surface need each screen as a component inside the host's own window: Slint 1.18 has no `ComponentContainer` unless experimental features are on. `generate_embedded` prints `component <Name>Screen inherits Rectangle` (not exported), with the screen's `label` as `accessible-label`, the screen box filling that rectangle, and the component's preferred size taken from the box. A `menu` is refused, because a `MenuBar` is legal only in a `Window` (Slint 1.18). `generate`, `weft slint` and `import_slint` stay on the `Window` form, so there is no new `weft.json` key. Done when every corpus screen without a menu compiles inside a host `Window`, two of them compile in one file, the menu screen is refused and still generates as a `Window`, and the existing goldens are unchanged.

**Execution plan.** (1) SPEC §9 "To Slint" and the `menu` row, AGENT-SPEC §5, and the crate README, in the same change as the code. (2) `generate_embedded` in `crates/weft-slint` sharing the generator with `generate`. (3) `tests/slint.rs`: every corpus screen and the controls fixture either compiles inside a host window or is refused only for a menu; `login` keeps its label and preferred size; `menu` is the only refusal; two screens compile as one file. (4) `cargo fmt`, `cargo test -p weft-slint --test slint`, clippy on the crate.

Result: https://github.com/pyrlyn/weft/pull/131. `generate_embedded` returns the component name, the sorted `std-widgets` imports and the component source. The `Window` goldens are unchanged.

### T69.2b. A canvas of embedded screens

Next slice of T69 milestone 2. `compose` assembles `generate_embedded` components into one `.slint` file whose only export is `Canvas`, a `Window`. Each frame is placed at `x + pan-x`, `y + pan-y`, with `width * zoom`, so a host moves the frames without regenerating the file. A repeated component name, or a position or width that is not a finite positive width, is an error. Done when two corpus screens compile in one canvas and a headless render paints both frames, and the `Window` goldens stay unchanged.

**Execution plan.** (1) SPEC §9, AGENT-SPEC §5 and the crate README, with the code. (2) `compose` in `crates/weft-slint`. (3) Tests: a duplicate name and a non-finite or empty width are refused; `login` and `account` compile as `Canvas` and a software-renderer snapshot has ink in both frames. (4) `cargo fmt`, `cargo test -p weft-slint --test slint`, clippy.

Result: `compose` writes the import line, each component once, and `Canvas` with `pan-x`, `pan-y` and `zoom`. Rulers, selection, keyboard navigation and pointer pan stay open.
